Concrete test cases derived from each user story's acceptance criteria. IDs group by feature area; **Story** column links back to the source acceptance criterion. Priority: **P1** = release-blocking, **P2** = should-have, **P3** = exploratory/structural-only (per FR priority in the Requirements Document).

---

## Auth

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-AUTH-001 | Login with valid credentials | Registered user exists | POST `/auth/login` with correct email+password | 200; access+refresh token issued; redirected to default org/project view | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-002 | Login with invalid credentials | — | POST `/auth/login` with wrong password | 401; generic "invalid credentials" message; body identical to unknown-email case (no enumeration) | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-003 | Login, single-org user | User has exactly 1 OrgMembership | Login | That org auto-selected, no picker shown. **Mechanism note (2026-09-13, [ADR-0064](../adr/0064-dash-3-dashboard-org-list-and-chooser.md)/DASH-3):** the redirect now lands on `/dashboard` first, which bounces onward to the org immediately (TC-DASH-007/013) — the end-user outcome asserted here is unchanged | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-004 | Login, multi-org user | User has 2+ OrgMemberships | Login | Org picker shown. **Mechanism note (2026-09-13, [ADR-0064](../adr/0064-dash-3-dashboard-org-list-and-chooser.md)/DASH-3):** the picker now lives at `/dashboard` itself ("Select an organization" card list, TC-DASH-009), not the retired `/orgs/pick`/`OrgPicker` — the end-user outcome asserted here is unchanged | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-005 | Password never logged/stored plaintext | — | Inspect DB row and application logs after signup/login | `password_hash` is argon2, no plaintext anywhere | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-006 | Silent refresh on access-token expiry | Valid refresh cookie present, access token expired | Call `GET /auth/me` (401), frontend interceptor calls `POST /auth/refresh`, retries original request once | New access token obtained transparently; retried `GET /auth/me` succeeds; no forced re-login | P1 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-007 | Refresh with revoked token | Refresh token revoked directly via DB fixture (`revoked_at` set — no logout/admin-revoke route exists yet, see AUTH-2 scope plan) | POST `/auth/refresh` | 401 `invalid_refresh_token`; no new token issued; frontend redirects to `/login` | P1 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-008 | Refresh tokens are individually revocable | 2 active sessions (2 `RefreshToken` rows) for same user | Revoke session A's refresh token via DB fixture | Session A's next refresh 401s; session B's refresh still succeeds normally | P2 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-018 | Refresh token is single-use (rotation) | Valid, unused refresh token | Call `POST /auth/refresh` once (succeeds, new cookie set), then present the *original* (now rotated-out) token again | First call 200s with a new token; second call 401s even though the original token had not otherwise expired or been explicitly revoked | P1 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-019 | Refresh rejected once token itself expires | Refresh token with `expires_at` in the past (fixture-seeded) | POST `/auth/refresh` | 401 `invalid_refresh_token`; no new token issued | P1 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-020 | Refresh rejected with no cookie at all | No `refresh_token` cookie sent | POST `/auth/refresh` | 401 `invalid_refresh_token` (same generic body as revoked/expired — no distinct code) | P2 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-021 | Refresh rejected once org access is lost | User's only `OrgMembership` transitions from `active` to `suspended` after login, refresh token itself still valid | POST `/auth/refresh` | 403 `no_active_organization`; refresh token **not** revoked by this rejection (a later refresh succeeds again if membership is reactivated before the token's `expires_at`) | P1 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-022 | Rotation inherits original session's absolute expiry | Token refreshed 3 times in a row (3 rotations) | Inspect the 4th-generation token's `expires_at` | Equal to the 1st-generation token's `expires_at` (copied forward each rotation), not `now + 30d` from the most recent rotation | P2 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-023 | `GET /auth/me` returns current actor identity | Valid access token | GET `/auth/me` | 200; `{actor_id, email, actor_type}` matches the authenticated `User` | P2 | AUTH-2 |
| TC-AUTH-009 | Logout revokes current session | Active session | POST `/auth/logout`, then POST `/auth/refresh` with the same (now-revoked) cookie | `/auth/logout` returns 204, `RefreshToken.revoked_reason="logout"`; the subsequent `/auth/refresh` call 401s `invalid_refresh_token`; frontend's token store cleared and redirected to `/login` regardless of the logout call's own success | P1 | AUTH-3 |
| TC-AUTH-024 | Logout with no refresh cookie is a no-op success | Active session, valid access token, `refresh_token` cookie absent/already cleared | POST `/auth/logout` with no cookie sent | 204; no `RefreshToken` row touched; response still clears the (already-absent) cookie | P2 | AUTH-3 |
| TC-AUTH-025 | Logout with already-revoked/rotated-out cookie is a no-op success | Refresh token already revoked (via a prior logout, or rotated out by `/auth/refresh`) | POST `/auth/logout` with the dead cookie | 204; no error, no double-revocation side effect (CAS `rowcount` is 0, row untouched beyond its existing `revoked_at`) | P2 | AUTH-3 |
| TC-AUTH-026 | Logout doesn't revoke a different session's refresh token | 2 active sessions (2 `RefreshToken` rows) for the same user | Logout session A | Session A's cookie now 401s on `/auth/refresh`; session B's refresh token still succeeds normally | P1 | AUTH-3 |
| TC-AUTH-027 | Logout rejected without a valid access token | No/expired/malformed bearer token | POST `/auth/logout` | 401 `invalid_token` (same generic body `GET /auth/me` uses via `get_current_actor`); no revocation attempted | P2 | AUTH-3 |
| TC-AUTH-010 | AIAgent bearer auth attributes actor correctly | AIAgent with issued, non-revoked API key | Call `GET /auth/me` with `Authorization: Bearer <raw key>` | 200; `{actor_id, agent_name, actor_type: "ai_agent"}` resolves to the AIAgent, not any User (mechanism-level proof — see AUTH-4 scope plan §1 for why a real business route isn't used here, none exist yet) | P2 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-011 | AIAgent blocked from Approval-permission route | AIAgent authenticated | Call `/test-plans/{id}/approve` | 403, regardless of role bundle | P1 | AUTH-4 / RBAC-5 |
| TC-AUTH-012 | org_admin issues/revokes AIAgent credential | Human User with `ai_agent.create`/`.update` in `org_id` (fixture-seeded RoleAssignment) | POST `/orgs/{org_id}/agents`, then POST `.../revoke` | Create: 201, `api_key` (raw `tnx_agent_...`) shown once, `key_prefix` also returned; Revoke: 200; a subsequent `GET /auth/me` call using the revoked raw key → 401 `invalid_token` | P2 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-028 | Revoked AIAgent key rejected before any state update | AIAgent key revoked via fixture (`revoked_at` set) | `GET /auth/me` with that key | 401 `invalid_token` (generic body, same shape as human path); `AIAgent.last_used_at` unchanged (rejection happens at the lookup, before update) | P1 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-029 | Key-prefix-narrowed lookup still verifies the full secret | Two AIAgent rows exist; craft a bearer value with agent A's `key_prefix` but agent B's (or a random) secret segment | `GET /auth/me` with the crafted value | 401 `invalid_token` — prefix match alone must not authenticate; argon2 verify against the prefix-matched candidate's `key_hash` must fail | P1 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-030 | `last_used_at` updates on every successful agent authentication | AIAgent key valid, `last_used_at` currently NULL | Call `GET /auth/me` twice, a few seconds apart | After call 1: `last_used_at` set (was NULL); after call 2: `last_used_at` advances to the later timestamp, not left at call 1's value | P2 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-031 | AIAgent cannot issue or revoke its own (or any) credential | AIAgent authenticated, `RoleAssignment` fixture-seeded to (incorrectly) grant `ai_agent.create`/`.update` anyway | POST `/orgs/{org_id}/agents` and `.../revoke` using the AIAgent's own bearer key | Both 403 `actor_forbidden`, unconditionally — human-only gate rejects before/independent of the permission check (NFR-17) | P1 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-032 | Org-scoped agent route: no membership vs. membership-without-permission | (a) Human User with zero `OrgMembership` in `org_id`; (b) Human User with active `OrgMembership` in `org_id` but no `ai_agent.create` grant | POST `/orgs/{org_id}/agents` as (a), then as (b) | (a) 404 `not_found`; (b) 403 — the two must not be conflated (NFR-19) | P1 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-033 | AC2 proof: AIAgent lacking the required permission → 403, same RBAC path as a human | AIAgent with a fixture-seeded `RoleAssignment` granting some other permission but not `ai_agent.update`, in `org_id` | Call `.../revoke` using the AIAgent's bearer key | 403 — same generic permission-denied shape a human `User` lacking `ai_agent.update` would get on the same route (not the human-only-gate's `actor_forbidden`; this proves the permission check itself, isolated from TC-AUTH-031's human-only gate) | P1 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-034 | `has_permission` org-wide grant recognized (AUTH-4's own RBAC-matrix slice) | Actor (human or AIAgent) with an org-wide `RoleAssignment` (`project_id = null`) granting `ai_agent.update`, fixture-seeded directly | Call `.../revoke` | 403 does **not** occur — permission recognized without any project-scoped grant present, proving the org-wide resolution branch works standalone | P2 | AUTH-4 |
| TC-AUTH-035 | `GET /auth/me/orgs` returns only active-status org memberships, human-only | User with a mix of `active`/`suspended`/`invited` `OrgMembership` rows across 3 orgs | Call `GET /auth/me/orgs`; also call it with an AIAgent bearer key; also call it unauthenticated | User call: 200, `orgs` lists only the `active`-status org, `OrgSummary` shape matches `LoginResponse.orgs`; AIAgent call: 403 `actor_forbidden`; unauthenticated call: 401 | P1 | SHELL-6 |
| TC-AUTH-013 | Login rejected, zero org memberships | User exists, no OrgMembership rows at all | POST `/auth/login` with correct credentials | 403 `no_active_organization`; no token issued | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-014 | Login rejected, only suspended/invited memberships | User has 1 `suspended` + 1 `invited` OrgMembership, none `active` | POST `/auth/login` with correct credentials | 403 `no_active_organization` | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-015 | Suspended/invited memberships excluded from org list | User has 1 `active` + 1 `suspended` OrgMembership | Login | `org_context: "auto"`; `orgs` contains only the active org, suspended org absent | P1 | AUTH-1 |
| TC-AUTH-016 | Login throttled after 5 failed attempts | 5 prior failed logins for same `(client_ip, email)` within 15 min | 6th login attempt for that pair | 429 `rate_limited`, regardless of whether attempt 6's credentials are correct | P1 | AUTH-1 / NFR-11 |
| TC-AUTH-017 | Successful login resets throttle counter | 3 failed attempts, then 1 successful login, same pair | 5 further failed attempts after the success | 429 triggers only after 5 new post-reset failures, not immediately on the next one | P2 | AUTH-1 / NFR-11 |

## RBAC & Multi-Tenancy

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-RBAC-001 | First-ever signup bootstraps org | Fresh instance, zero orgs | POST `/auth/signup` | 200; Organization+User+OrgMembership(active)+org-wide org_admin RoleAssignment created; tokens issued (`org_context: "auto"`) | P1 | RBAC-1 |
| TC-RBAC-002 | Cross-org data isolation | 2 orgs exist, each with a Project/Release/Requirement/TestCase | org_admin of Org A requests a resource id belonging to Org B | 404 (not 403, not data) | P1 | RBAC-1 / NFR-1 — **fully covered as of ADMIN-2/REQ-1** (see TC-PROJ-012 for `Project`, TC-PROJ-016 for `Release`, and **TC-ADMIN-006** for `Requirement` (direct resolver depth) and `TestCase` (multi-hop resolver depth), all reusing the `/orgs/{org_id}/agents*` 404-vs-403 pattern per ADR-0015/[ADR-0017](../adr/0017-project-creation-flow.md)/[ADR-0019](../adr/0019-release-creation-flow.md)/[ADR-0022](../adr/0022-generic-crud-router-factory.md)) — this row's original "Requirement/TestCase remain blocked until their own CRUD routes exist" note is now stale and superseded by ADMIN-2's factory landing |
| TC-RBAC-003 | Org slug uniqueness | Org "acme" exists | Authenticated org_admin: POST `/orgs` with slug "acme" | Rejected, 422 (not 409 — reserved for signup-closed, ADR-0016) | P2 | RBAC-1 |
| TC-RBAC-020 | Concurrent first-signup race is serialized | Fresh instance, zero orgs | Fire 2 concurrent `POST /auth/signup` requests | Exactly one `Organization` row exists afterward — `pg_advisory_xact_lock` prevents both succeeding | P2 | RBAC-1 |
| TC-RBAC-021 | Signup closes after bootstrap | 1 org already exists | POST `/auth/signup` | 409 `signup_closed`; no new User/Organization rows created | P1 | RBAC-1 |
| TC-RBAC-022 | Existing org_admin creates a second, isolated org | User is org_admin of Org A (org-wide RoleAssignment) | POST `/orgs` with `{name, slug}` | 201; new Organization created; creator gets OrgMembership(active)+org-wide org_admin RoleAssignment in it; Org A's own OrgMembership/RoleAssignment rows unchanged | P1 | RBAC-1 |
| TC-RBAC-023 | `has_permission_in_any_org` ignores project-scoped-only grants | Actor's only `organization.create`-granting RoleAssignment is project-scoped (`project_id` non-null) | POST `/orgs` | 403 — a project-scoped grant does not satisfy the any-org, org-wide-only gate | P2 | RBAC-1 |
| TC-RBAC-004 | Invite member by email | org_admin authenticated | POST invite with email | OrgMembership created, status `invited` | P1 | RBAC-2 |
| TC-RBAC-005 | Invited user completes signup | Pending invite exists | User signs up via invite link | OrgMembership status becomes `active` | P1 | RBAC-2 |
| TC-RBAC-006 | Suspend member blocks access, keeps RoleAssignment | Active member with RoleAssignment | org_admin suspends member; suspended user calls any API | 403/401 on all calls; RoleAssignment rows still present in DB | P1 | RBAC-2 |
| TC-RBAC-007 | Multi-org membership | — | Same user gets OrgMembership in 2 orgs | Both memberships valid independently | P2 | RBAC-2 |
| TC-RBAC-008 | Org-wide role grant | RoleAssignment with `project_id = null` granting `project.read`/`.update` | Grantee calls `GET`/`PATCH /projects/{id}` against 2+ distinct Projects in the org | 200 on every project in the org, not just one — real HTTP proof via the [ADR-0021](../adr/0021-role-assignment-creation-flow.md) fix, not a `has_permission()`-only assertion | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-009 | Project-scoped role grant | RoleAssignment scoped to Project A only | Grantee calls `GET`/`PATCH /projects/{A.id}`, then the same against Project B (same org, no separate grant) | Project A: 200; Project B: 403 (no implicit access outside the scoped project) | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-010 | No RoleAssignment → no implicit access | User has OrgMembership (any status) but zero RoleAssignment rows anywhere in the org | User calls `GET /projects/{id}` for a Project in that org | 403 | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-011 | AIAgent RoleAssignment | AIAgent actor, `RoleAssignment` created via `POST /orgs/{org_id}/role-assignments` | AIAgent authenticates (bearer key) and calls `GET`/`PATCH /projects/{id}` per the same org-wide/project-scoped/no-access matrix as TC-RBAC-008/009/010 | AIAgent's permission set resolves exactly like a human User's would — same outcomes, same `has_permission` join chain (07 principle #6) | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-024 | Create RoleAssignment, org-wide | org_admin authenticated, has `role_assignment.create` in `org_id`, target actor is an org member | POST `/orgs/{org_id}/role-assignments` with `{actor_id, role_id}`, `project_id` omitted | 201; row created with `project_id = null` | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-025 | Create RoleAssignment, project-scoped | Same as above, Project exists in `org_id` | POST with `{actor_id, role_id, project_id}` | 201; row created with the given `project_id` | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-026 | Create-endpoint 404-vs-403 boundary | (a) caller with zero OrgMembership in `org_id`; (b) caller with membership but no `role_assignment.create` | POST as (a), then as (b) | (a) 404; (b) 403 — never conflated (NFR-19 pattern reused) | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-027 | Cross-org `role_id`/`project_id` rejected as validation error | `role_id` belongs to a custom Role in a different org; separately, `project_id` belongs to a Project in a different org | POST with each bad field in turn | Both 422 (never 404 — [ADR-0021](../adr/0021-role-assignment-creation-flow.md), caller already proved membership in `org_id`) | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-028 | Unknown `actor_id` rejected | `actor_id` doesn't resolve to any `Actor` row | POST with that `actor_id` | 422 | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-029 | Duplicate RoleAssignment rejected | Identical `(actor_id, org_id, project_id, role_id)` grant already exists | POST the same payload again | 422 (unique-constraint violation, not a silent second row) | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-030 | User-actor OrgMembership precondition | Target `actor_id` resolves to a `User` with zero OrgMembership rows in `org_id` | POST a grant for that actor | 422 — a `RoleAssignment` for a non-member `User` is rejected | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-031 | Any-status membership satisfies the precondition | Target `User` has a `suspended`-status (not `active`) OrgMembership in `org_id` | POST a grant for that actor | 201 — any status satisfies the gate, not active-only (RBAC-2's suspension model keeps roles manageable) | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-032 | AIAgent actor skips the membership gate | Target `actor_id` resolves to an `AIAgent` (no OrgMembership row exists or ever will) | POST a grant for that AIAgent | 201 — gate not applied to AIAgent actors | P1 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-033 | List RoleAssignments for an org | Org has 1 org-wide + 1 project-scoped grant; a different org has its own grant | GET `/orgs/{org_id}/role-assignments` | 200; both of this org's rows returned, the other org's row absent | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-034 | List-endpoint 404-vs-403 boundary | (a) caller with zero OrgMembership in `org_id`; (b) caller with membership but no `role_assignment.read` | GET as (a), then as (b) | (a) 404; (b) 403 | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-035 | Regression: project creator's own project-scoped role now works | Actor creates a Project (PROJ-1), auto-granted only a project-scoped `test_manager` `RoleAssignment` (no org-wide grant) | Same actor calls `GET`/`PATCH /projects/{id}` on the project they just created | 200 — proves the [ADR-0021](../adr/0021-role-assignment-creation-flow.md) fix, not just the new endpoint; would have 403'd before this story | P1 | RBAC-3 / PROJ-1 |
| TC-RBAC-036 | List roles for an org (UI dropdown source) | Org has 1 custom Role; a different org has its own custom Role | GET `/orgs/{org_id}/roles` | 200; all 5 RBAC-4 system roles present + this org's custom role; the other org's custom role absent | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-037 | Roles-endpoint 404-vs-403 boundary | (a) caller with zero OrgMembership in `org_id`; (b) caller with membership but no `role.read` | GET as (a), then as (b) | (a) 404; (b) 403 | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-038 | Role Assignments UI: grant org-wide via the form | org_admin on `OrgHome`, role dropdown populated from `GET /orgs/{org_id}/roles` | Open "New Role Assignment", paste actor id, select a role, submit with scope left at "Org-wide" | Grant appears in the table with scope "Org-wide"; `POST` body has no `project_id` | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-039 | Role Assignments UI: grant project-scoped via the form | Same as above, a Project exists in the org | Switch scope to "Project-scoped", enter a project id, submit | Grant appears in the table with scope "Project {id}"; `POST` body includes `project_id` | P2 | RBAC-3 |
| TC-RBAC-012 | System roles seeded on org creation | New org just created | Query available roles | `org_admin`, `test_manager`, `tester`, `auditor`, `ai_agent_scoped` all present, `is_system_role=true` | P1 | RBAC-4 |
| TC-RBAC-013 | Custom role creation | org_admin authenticated | Create a custom Role scoped to the org | Role created with `org_id` set, usable in RoleAssignment | P2 | RBAC-4 |
| TC-RBAC-014 | ai_agent_scoped never has approval permission | Seed migration applied | Inspect `ai_agent_scoped`'s RolePermission rows | `test_plan.approve` absent | P1 | RBAC-5 |
| TC-RBAC-015 | Reject adding approval permission to an AIAgent-targeted role | Custom role assigned to an AIAgent | Attempt to add `test_plan.approve` to that role via admin UI/API | Rejected | P1 | RBAC-5 |
| TC-RBAC-016 | Seed migration idempotent on re-run | Seed migration already applied | Apply the same migration a second time | `role`/`permission`/`role_permission` row counts unchanged; no error | P1 | RBAC-4 |
| TC-RBAC-017 | Partial unique index rejects duplicate system role | Seed migration applied | Insert a second `Role` row with `name='org_admin'`, `org_id=NULL` directly | Rejected by DB constraint | P1 | RBAC-4 |
| TC-RBAC-018 | org_admin bundle equals full Permission catalog | Seed migration applied | Count `org_admin`'s `RolePermission` rows vs. total `Permission` row count | Counts equal — every seeded permission is granted | P1 | RBAC-4 |
| TC-RBAC-019 | Downgrade removes roles, keeps Permission catalog | Seed migration applied | Run `alembic downgrade -1` for the seed migration | 5 system `Role` rows and their `RolePermission` rows gone; `Permission` catalog rows still present | P2 | RBAC-4 |

## Project & Release

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-PROJ-001 | Create project | User has `project.create` in `org_id` | POST `/orgs/{org_id}/projects` with name | 201; Project created, scoped to `org_id` | P1 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-002 | No orphaned assets outside a project | — | Attempt to create Requirement/TestSuite/TestPlan without `project_id` | Schema-enforced (non-nullable `project_id` FK) **and** now live-executable via the API — **covered as of ADMIN-2** for `Requirement` specifically by **TC-ADMIN-010** (`POST /requirements` with no `project_id` in the body → `422`); `TestSuite`/`TestPlan` share the same generic-factory `scope_field` mechanism (ADR-0022) but aren't each individually enumerated as their own test case, per Test Plan §6's "representative entity per depth, not exhaustive per entity" posture. This row's original "no create route exists yet" / execution-deferred note ([ADR-0017](../adr/0017-project-creation-flow.md)) is now stale and superseded by ADMIN-2's factory landing | P1 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-003 | Set standards_profile explicitly at creation | Org exists | POST with `standards_profile: "ISTQB-CTFL-v4.0.1 + ISO29119-3"` | Project created with that exact value, org default not consulted | P2 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-004 | Create release | Project exists, `release.create` held | POST `/projects/{project_id}/releases` with version_label/target_date | 201; Release created, scoped to `project_id` | P1 | PROJ-2 |
| TC-PROJ-005 | Query cycles for a release | Release has 2 linked TestCycles, each with executions | GET `/releases/{id}/test-cycles` | 200; returns both cycles, each with its own `TestExecution`s nested (not a flat merged list) — one call answers "what was tested for release X" | P1 | PROJ-2 |
| TC-PROJ-006 | Inherit standards_profile from org default | `Organization.default_standards_profile` set; create payload omits `standards_profile` | POST without the field | New Project's `standards_profile` equals the org's default | P2 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-007 | Explicit null also inherits org default (amended [ADR-0059](../adr/0059-project-generic-admin-create.md), 2026-09-12 — originally asserted the opposite, see docstring) | `Organization.default_standards_profile` set (non-null) | POST with `standards_profile: null` explicitly | New Project's `standards_profile` equals the org default, same as if the field had been omitted — the omitted-vs-explicit-null distinction no longer applies to create (`EntityForm`, the generic admin surface, cannot express "omit this key") | P2 | PROJ-1, amended ADR-0059 |
| TC-PROJ-008 | Update standards_profile via PATCH | Project exists, actor has `project.update` | PATCH `/projects/{id}` with a new `standards_profile` | New value persists; omitting the field on a later PATCH leaves it unchanged (no org-default fallback on update) | P2 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-009 | Creator auto-assigned project-scoped test_manager role | org_admin creates a Project | Inspect `RoleAssignment` rows after creation | New row: `role=test_manager`, `project_id`=new Project, `org_id`=Project's org; creator's pre-existing org-wide `org_admin` `RoleAssignment` unchanged (both coexist) | P1 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-010 | Project name uniqueness is org-scoped | Project "Alpha" exists in Org X | POST another "Alpha" in Org X, then POST "Alpha" in Org Y | Org X duplicate → 422; Org Y same name → 201 (uniqueness is `(org_id, name)`, not global) | P2 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-011 | 404-vs-403 boundary on project creation | (a) actor with zero `OrgMembership` in `org_id`; (b) actor with membership but no `project.create` | POST `/orgs/{org_id}/projects` as (a), then as (b) | (a) 404; (b) 403 — never conflated (NFR-19 pattern reused) | P1 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-012 | Cross-org GET/PATCH rejected | Project P belongs to Org A; actor is a member of Org B only | GET `/projects/{P.id}` and PATCH same, authenticated as the Org B member | Both 404 — first concrete proof of TC-RBAC-002/NFR-1 against a real tenant-scoped resource | P1 | PROJ-1 / RBAC-1 (TC-RBAC-002) |
| TC-PROJ-013 | GET/PATCH membership-without-permission → 403 | Actor is a member of the Project's org but lacks `project.read`/`.update` | GET, then PATCH, the Project | Both 403 — distinct from TC-PROJ-012's 404 (member vs. non-member) | P2 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-014 | Releases sortable by target_date, NULLS LAST both directions | 4 Releases in a Project: 3 with distinct `target_date`s, 1 with `null` | GET `/projects/{id}/releases?sort=target_date&order=asc`, then `...&order=desc` | `asc`: dated releases ascending, `null` row last. `desc`: dated releases descending, `null` row **still last** (not first — proves the pin isn't relying on the query engine's per-direction default) | P2 | PROJ-2 |
| TC-PROJ-015 | test-cycles query triple-permission gate | Actor is a member of the Release's org; holds exactly 2 of {`release.read`, `test_cycle.read`, `test_execution.read`} at a time (3 sub-cases, each missing a different one) | GET `/releases/{id}/test-cycles` under each sub-case | 403 in all 3 sub-cases, never a partial/degraded 200 — proves the gate requires all three, not any one or two | P1 | PROJ-2 |
| TC-PROJ-016 | Cross-org 404 on Release routes | Release R belongs to a Project in Org A; actor is a member of Org B only | GET `/releases/{R.id}` and GET `/releases/{R.id}/test-cycles`, authenticated as the Org B member | Both 404 — extends TC-RBAC-002/NFR-1 to `Release`, same pattern TC-PROJ-012 established for `Project` | P1 | PROJ-2 / RBAC-1 (TC-RBAC-002) |
| TC-PROJ-017 | test_manager can create/list Releases without org_admin | Actor holds only a `test_manager` `RoleAssignment` (no `org_admin` anywhere); RBAC bundle-extension migration applied | POST `/projects/{id}/releases`, then GET `/projects/{id}/releases` | Both succeed (201, 200) — proves the `test_manager` bundle extension ([ADR-0019](../adr/0019-release-creation-flow.md)) actually applied, not just that `org_admin` can reach these routes | P2 | PROJ-2 |
| TC-PROJ-018 | Project list survives navigating away and back | A Project was created via `OrgHome`'s "New Project" modal | Click into the Project's own detail page, then navigate back to `OrgHome` (a route change, not a page reload) | The Project still appears in the list — `OrgHome` fetches it via `GET /projects?org_id=` (ADR-0022) on every mount rather than relying on local component state, which a remount would have reset (bug fixed 2026-09-07) | P1 | PROJ-1 |
| TC-PROJ-019 | ~~Project table shows the full ID/Name/Standards profile/Actions column set~~ **Superseded [ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md):** the bespoke `ProjectsPage` table (and its ID column) no longer exists — the generic admin surface's own column set (Organization/Name/Standards-profile/Actions) is covered once, generically, by `EntityTable.test.tsx`, not a per-entity TC | 1+ Projects exist in the org | ~~**Revised 2026-09-09 (PROJ-4, [ADR-0047](../adr/0047-proj-4-projects-page-sidebar-entry.md)):** Load `OrgHome`/Dashboard the dedicated `/orgs/:orgId/projects` page~~ | ~~Table header is exactly `ID`, `Name`, `Standards profile`, and an unlabeled Actions column — the ordered full set, not a subset~~ | P3 | DASH-2, superseded ADR-0060 |
| TC-PROJ-020 | ~~Edit modal edits name and standards_profile together, persists~~ **Superseded [ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md):** edit is a route now, not a modal — generic `EntityForm`'s own edit-submit behavior is covered once, generically, not a per-entity TC | 2+ Projects exist, one with a non-null `standards_profile` | ~~Click that row's `Edit`, change both `Name` and `Standards profile`, `Save`~~ | ~~`PATCH /projects/{id}` called once with both fields in the same body; modal closes; the row shows both new values, the other row's values unchanged~~ | P1 | DASH-2, superseded ADR-0060 |
| TC-PROJ-021 | Delete via confirm modal calls DELETE /projects/{id}, permission-gated | 2+ Projects exist; actor is `org_admin` (holds `project.delete`) | Click one row's `Delete`, confirm in the modal. Second half: repeat as an actor holding only `test_manager` (no `project.delete`) | Positive half: `DELETE /projects/{id}` called for that project's id only; the row disappears from the table; the other row remains. Negative half: `403` surfaces as an alert inside the still-open modal, row NOT removed. **Resolved (2026-09-07, [ADR-0040](../adr/0040-role-assignment-project-cascade-delete.md)):** verification initially found the positive half only succeeded against a project seeded directly at the DB layer — a Project created through the app's own `POST /orgs/{id}/projects` route always `409`'d instead (`role_assignment.project_id`'s `ON DELETE RESTRICT`). Fixed same-day (FK now `CASCADE`) — `test_delete_project_created_via_api_cascades_the_creator_role_assignment` now proves the create-then-delete path succeeds end to end, including the cascade itself. **[ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md):** unaffected — same route, same modal-confirm pattern, now via the generic surface | P1 | DASH-2 |

(not "No projects yet.")~~ | P2 | DASH-2, superseded ADR-0060 |
| TC-PROJ-023 | ~~Sort by ID/Name toggles ascending/descending independently per column~~ **Superseded [ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md):** sort is the generic ADR-0056 click-to-cycle mechanism now (any sortable column, not just ID/Name) — covered once, generically, by `TC-ADMIN-039` | 2+ Projects with distinguishable names/ids | ~~Click the `Name` header once, then again; then click the `ID` header~~ | ~~First click sorts one direction, second click reverses it (both proven via actual rendered row order); clicking `ID` resets to ascending on `ID`, not carrying over `Name`'s direction~~ | P2 | DASH-2, superseded ADR-0060 |
| TC-PROJ-024 | ~~Pagination shows exactly 10 rows per page, no control below that threshold~~ **Superseded [ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md):** pagination is server-side now, default page size 25 (not 10) — the generic mechanism is covered once, generically, by `container/Table.test.tsx`/`TC-DS-009..018` | 12 Projects exist | ~~Load the table (**revised 2026-09-09, PROJ-4:** now the dedicated `/orgs/:orgId/projects` page, was `OrgHome`/Dashboard); click the page-2 control~~ | ~~Page 1 shows exactly its first 10, none of the last 2; page 2 shows the remaining 2, none of the first 10. A ≤10-project fixture renders no pagination control at all~~ | P2 | DASH-2, superseded ADR-0060 |
| TC-PROJ-025 | Sidebar "Projects" nav item renders (with a `fa-solid fa-folder` icon, **amended 2026-09-09**), navigates to the dedicated page, and is active-highlighted there (not "Dashboard") | Authenticated user with `orgId` in context | Look at the sidebar; click "Projects"; on the resulting page, inspect both nav items' active state | Item renders between "Dashboard" and "Members" with a folder icon (matching `ProjectCountWidget`'s own); click lands on `/orgs/:orgId/projects`, heading reads "Projects"; "Projects" carries the active class there, "Dashboard" does not | P1 | PROJ-4 |
| TC-PROJ-026 | "Dashboard" no longer hosts the Project table; its Project-count widget links to the new page | Authenticated user with `orgId` in context, 1+ Projects exist | Load `/orgs/:orgId` (Dashboard); inspect for a "New Project" button/table; inspect the Project-count widget's container | No "New Project" button, no Project table, no "No projects yet."/"No projects match your search." text anywhere on Dashboard; the Project-count widget is wrapped in a link to `/orgs/:orgId/projects` | P2 | PROJ-4 |
| TC-PROJ-027 | Row's own name links to `ProjectDetail` — the one genuinely Project-specific `EntityTable` behavior (`detailPath`/`detailLinkField`, [ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md)) | 1+ Projects exist in the org | Load `/orgs/:orgId/projects`; click a row's name (not its Edit/Delete action) | A real `<a href="/projects/{id}">` navigates to that Project's `ProjectDetail` page — proven via an actual click+navigation, not just an `href` attribute check (root `CLAUDE.md`'s own "prove a nav target with a real click" precedent, SHELL-9/ADR-0050) | P2 | ADR-0060 |

## Requirement & Test Case Authoring

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-REQ-001 | Capture a requirement | Project exists, `requirement.create` held | POST with title/description/source/external_ref | Requirement created, scoped to project | P1 | REQ-1 — `title` added to schema by [ADR-0025](../adr/0025-requirement-title-field.md); the create route itself (project-scoping, permission gate, 404-vs-403 boundary) was already delivered by ADMIN-2 |
| TC-REQ-002 | Search requirements | Multiple requirements exist | Search by title substring (`?q=`) and by external_ref (`?external_ref=`, exact) | Correct subset returned for each; the two params tested as distinct classes (a substring passed to `?external_ref=` must not match) | P2 | REQ-1 — `title`'s inclusion in `search_fields` per [ADR-0025](../adr/0025-requirement-title-field.md); `?q=`/`filter_fields` mechanism itself is ADR-0022's |
| TC-REQ-003 | Direct TestCase authoring (no TestCondition) | Requirement exists | POST test-case with `test_condition_id=null`, linked via RequirementTestCaseLink | TestCase traceable to Requirement without any TestCondition existing | P1 | REQ-2 |
| TC-REQ-004 | Add and reorder TestSteps | TestCase exists | Add 3 steps, reorder | Steps independently editable, sequence persists | P1 | REQ-2 |
| TC-REQ-005 | TestCondition authoring | Requirement exists, actor holds `test_condition.create` | POST `/requirements/{id}/test-conditions` (description, priority) | `201`; both the TestCondition row and a RequirementTestConditionLink row exist (asserted by querying the link table, not just the response body) | P1 | REQ-3 |
| TC-REQ-006 | TestCase via TestCondition | TestCondition exists, actor holds `test_case.create` | POST `/test-conditions/{id}/test-cases` with `test_level_id`/`test_type_id` | `201`; both the TestCase row (`test_condition_id` set, `status=draft`) and a TestConditionTestCaseLink row exist; walking TestConditionTestCaseLink→TestCondition→RequirementTestConditionLink→Requirement reaches the exact originating Requirement | P1 | REQ-3 |
| TC-REQ-007 | Both authoring paths coexist | Same project; one TestCase created via REQ-2's direct path, one via REQ-3's TestCondition-mediated path | GET each TestCase | Both `200`; each shows the correct `test_condition_id` (`null` vs. set) and is reachable only via its own link table — verified at the API/DB layer (no traceability-view UI exists yet, FR-TRACE-1/2) | P1 | REQ-3 |
| TC-REQ-008 | Suite membership many-to-many | TestSuite A + TestSuite B (same project) + TestCase exist | POST `/test-suites/{A.id}/test-cases/{case.id}`, then POST `/test-suites/{B.id}/test-cases/{case.id}` | Both `201`; both `TestSuiteTestCase` rows exist afterward (queried directly), each suite's membership independent of the other | P1 | REQ-4 |
| TC-REQ-009 | Suite membership stays live pre-execution | TestSuite has 2 TestCases | GET `/test-suites/{id}/test-cases` (returns both), DELETE one, GET again (same test, sequenced) | First GET: both cases. DELETE: `204`. Second GET: only the remaining case — reflects current membership, not a stale/cached snapshot | P2 | REQ-4 |
| TC-REQ-010 | `test_manager` can author TestConditions/TestCases without `org_admin`/`tester` | Actor holds only a `test_manager` RoleAssignment; RBAC bundle-extension migration ([ADR-0028](../adr/0028-req3-test-condition-rigor-path-bespoke-routes.md)) applied | POST `/requirements/{id}/test-conditions`, then POST `/test-conditions/{id}/test-cases` | Both succeed (`201`) — proves the `test_manager` bundle extension actually applied, not just that `org_admin`/`tester` can reach these routes | P2 | REQ-3 |
| TC-REQ-011 | Generic TestCondition create route removed | — | POST `/test-conditions` (the pre-ADR-0028 generic factory route) | No longer accepts `POST`; `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /test-conditions/{id}` and `GET /test-conditions?requirement_id=` unaffected | P2 | REQ-3 |
| TC-REQ-012 | Cross-project add rejected | TestSuite in Project A; TestCase resolves to Project B (same org) — tested via at least 2 of the 3 ADR-0029 resolver branches (direct-link and TestCondition-mediated), not just one | POST `/test-suites/{A-suite.id}/test-cases/{case.id}` for each branch's TestCase | `422 validation_error` in every branch tested, never `404` (caller already proved org membership) | P1 | REQ-4 |
| TC-REQ-013 | Duplicate add is a conflict, not a validation error | TestCase already a member of TestSuite | POST the same `/test-suites/{id}/test-cases/{case_id}` pair again | `409 already_in_suite`, distinct from `422`; the first (non-duplicate) POST in the same test asserted `201` immediately prior | P1 | REQ-4 |
| TC-REQ-014 | Remove a non-member is 404, not idempotent-204 | TestCase was never added to TestSuite (or was already removed) | DELETE `/test-suites/{id}/test-cases/{case_id}` | `404` — deliberately asymmetric with `POST`'s `409`-on-already-true (ADR-0030) | P2 | REQ-4 |
| TC-REQ-015 | Standalone TestCase authoring (no Requirement, no TestCondition) | Project exists, `test_case.create` held | POST `/test-cases` (generic factory) with `project_id` + title/test_level_id/test_type_id | `201`; `test_condition_id` is `null`; no `RequirementTestCaseLink` row exists for the new case (queried directly, not inferred from the response) | P1 | REQ-5 |
| TC-REQ-016 | Standalone TestCase reachable via the project-scoped admin list | Project has 0, then 1+, standalone TestCases | GET `/test-cases?project_id=<id>` before and after creating one | Empty project: `200` empty list, not `404`. After create: the new case appears — proves the sidebar's pre-existing "Test cases" nav item (ADR-0051) now resolves to real data, not just that create succeeded | P2 | REQ-5 |
| TC-REQ-017 | Link an existing standalone TestCase to a Requirement | Standalone TestCase (no traceability) + same-project Requirement exist | POST `/test-cases/{id}/link-requirement` with `requirement_id`; then GET `/test-cases/{id}` (same test, sequenced) | POST: `201`; a `RequirementTestCaseLink` row exists (queried directly). GET: still `200` — proves the resolver's Requirement-link branch, not a stale `project_id` branch, now governs (create-then-link-then-read round trip, not a create/link-only assertion) | P1 | REQ-5 |
| TC-REQ-018 | Re-linking an already-linked TestCase is a conflict | Tested at both sub-classes: (a) TestCase with `test_condition_id` already set; (b) TestCase already linked via a prior `link-requirement` call | POST `/test-cases/{id}/link-requirement` with a valid `requirement_id`, for each sub-class | `409 already_linked_to_requirement` in both sub-classes, distinct from `422` | P2 | REQ-5 |
| TC-REQ-019 | Link-requirement cross-boundary rejection | Standalone TestCase in Project A/Org 1 | POST `/test-cases/{id}/link-requirement` with a `requirement_id` resolving to (a) a different org, (b) the same org but Project B | (a) `404` — existence-hiding, NFR-1, never leaks cross-org existence. (b) `422 validation_error` — same-org, cross-project rejection, never `404` (mirrors TC-REQ-012's own precedent for suite membership) | P1 | REQ-5 |

## Test Planning

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-PLAN-001 | Create test plan | Project exists, `test_plan.create` held | POST with identifier/scope/approach/staffing/schedule | Created with status `draft` | P1 | PLAN-1 |
| TC-PLAN-002 | Include suites in plan, coverage query | TestPlan + 2 TestSuites (same project) exist, one shared TestCase is a member of both | POST `/test-plans/{id}/test-suites/{suite_id}` for each suite, then GET `/test-plans/{id}/test-cases` | Both `TestPlanTestSuite` rows exist; coverage query returns the union of both suites' member TestCases, with the shared TestCase appearing exactly once (deduplicated) | P1 | PLAN-1 |
| TC-PLAN-003 | Status transitions | TestPlan in `draft`, actor holds `test_plan.update` | `PATCH` status draft→approved, then approved→superseded (both succeed); separately, a fresh `draft` TestPlan `PATCH`ed directly to `superseded` | draft→approved→superseded both succeed (200); draft→superseded direct is rejected `409 invalid_status_transition` | P1 | PLAN-1 |
| TC-PLAN-004 | Add, edit, delete entry/exit criteria | TestPlan exists | POST one criteria row per type (entry/exit/suspension/resumption), then GET `/entry-exit-criteria?test_plan_id=` to confirm listed, then PATCH one row's `condition_text`, then DELETE another | All 4 types listed against the plan after create; PATCH'd row reflects the edit on next GET; DELETE'd row absent on next GET | P1 | PLAN-2 |
| TC-PLAN-005 | Exit criteria visible with execution progress, one view, type-filtered | TestPlan with both an `exit`-type row and a non-`exit`-type row; TestCycle running under it, with ≥1 TestExecution | GET `/releases/{id}/test-cycles` | Response's matching `TestCycle` entry carries both its existing `executions` **and** a new `exit_criteria` array containing only the `exit`-type row — the non-`exit` row absent, not merely unasserted | P1 | PLAN-2 |
| TC-PLAN-012 | Exit criteria empty state on cycle view | TestCycle under a TestPlan with zero `exit`-type rows (plan may still have entry/suspension/resumption rows) | GET `/releases/{id}/test-cycles` | Matching cycle's `exit_criteria` is `[]`, never omitted or `null` | P2 | PLAN-2 |
| TC-PLAN-013 | Cycle view's exit criteria requires the new permission independently | Actor holds `release.read`+`test_cycle.read`+`test_execution.read` but not `entry_exit_criteria.read` | GET `/releases/{id}/test-cycles` | `403` — the pre-existing triple alone no longer suffices | P2 | PLAN-2 |
| TC-PLAN-006 | Create test cycle, then read back | TestPlan + Release + Environment exist (same project) | POST `/test-plans/{id}/test-cycles` with `release_id`/`environment_id`/`name`, then GET `/test-cycles/{id}` | `201`, linked to both plan and release; the immediate GET succeeds (proves no resolver gap, `backend/CLAUDE.md`) | P1 | PLAN-3 |
| TC-PLAN-007 | Create environment inline | Setting up a cycle, no environment yet | POST `/environments` then POST `/test-plans/{id}/test-cycles` with the returned `environment_id` (sequential calls, [ADR-0033](../adr/0033-plan3-test-cycle-creation-and-execution-scope-check.md)) | Environment created; cycle linked to it in the same flow | P2 | PLAN-3 |
| TC-PLAN-008 | Execution scope enforcement, both directions | Two `TestCase`s: one IS a member of a suite included in the plan, one is NOT | POST `/test-cycles/{id}/executions` for each `test_case_id` | Covered case → `201`; uncovered case → `422`, never `404`; covered case's membership additionally confirmed against `GET /test-plans/{id}/test-cases`'s own result set | P1 | PLAN-3 |
| TC-PLAN-015 | Cross-project TestCycle create rejected — release | Release in Project A; TestPlan in Project B (same org) | POST `/test-plans/{B-plan.id}/test-cycles` with `release_id = A-release.id` | `422 validation_error`, never `404` (caller already proved org membership) | P1 | PLAN-3 |
| TC-PLAN-016 | Cross-project TestCycle create rejected — environment | Environment in Project A; TestPlan in Project B (same org) | POST `/test-plans/{B-plan.id}/test-cycles` with `environment_id = A-environment.id` | `422 validation_error`, never `404` — tested independently of TC-PLAN-015 (a fix scoped to only one of the two fields must not pass this case) | P1 | PLAN-3 |
| TC-PLAN-017 | Generic TestExecution create route removed | — | POST `/test-executions` (the pre-ADR-0033 generic factory route) | No longer accepts `POST`; `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /test-executions/{id}` and `GET /test-executions?test_cycle_id=` unaffected | P2 | PLAN-3 |
| TC-PLAN-018 | Standalone `entry-exit-criteria`/`test-cycles` admin pages retired, unreachable by typed URL | Frontend build against `main` | Navigate to `/projects/{id}/admin/entry-exit-criteria` and `/projects/{id}/admin/test-cycles` directly by URL; separately confirm neither key appears in the sidebar's Test Planning group | Both render "Unknown admin entity" (the same existing render path any bad `:entity` key already gets — no redirect); sidebar shows only Test Plans/Releases under Test Planning | P1 | Retire-EntryExitCriteria-TestCycles |
| TC-PLAN-019 | Test Cycle inline Edit/Delete on `TestPlanDetail`, replacing the retired admin surface | TestPlan with one TestCycle | PATCH the cycle's `name`/`environment_id` via the new "Edit" button's modal, then DELETE via the new "Delete" button — both on `TestPlanDetail`, no navigation away | Edit: `200`, row reflects the new name/environment on next re-fetch, `test_plan_id`/`release_id` never sent (filtered from the form, not reassignable). Delete: `204`, row absent on next re-fetch, no confirmation modal (matches Entry/Exit Criteria's own no-confirm convention) | P1 | Retire-EntryExitCriteria-TestCycles |
| TC-PLAN-009 | Cross-project include rejected | TestPlan in Project A; TestSuite in Project B (same org) | POST `/test-plans/{A-plan.id}/test-suites/{B-suite.id}` | `422 validation_error`, never `404` (caller already proved org membership) | P1 | PLAN-1 |
| TC-PLAN-010 | Duplicate include is a conflict, not a validation error | TestSuite already included in TestPlan | POST the same `/test-plans/{id}/test-suites/{suite_id}` pair again | `409 already_included_in_plan`, distinct from `422`; the first (non-duplicate) POST in the same test asserted `201` immediately prior | P1 | PLAN-1 |
| TC-PLAN-011 | Remove a non-included suite is 404, not idempotent-204 | TestSuite was never included in TestPlan (or was already removed) | DELETE `/test-plans/{id}/test-suites/{suite_id}` | `404` — deliberately asymmetric with `POST`'s `409`-on-already-true (ADR-0031, mirrors ADR-0030) | P2 | PLAN-1 |

## Test Execution & Defects

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-EXEC-001 | Record execution result | TestCase in scope for active cycle, caller holds `test_execution.create` | `POST /test-cycles/{id}/executions` with `result=pass`, `actual_result` notes | `201`; row created with `executed_by_actor_id` = caller's own `actor_id`, `executed_at` set, immediately readable via `GET /test-executions/{id}` | P1 | EXEC-1 |
| TC-EXEC-002 | Live dashboard aggregation | Cycle has a mixed seeded set — at least one `pass`, one `fail`, one `blocked`, one `skipped` `TestExecution` | Call `GET /test-executions?test_cycle_id=&result=<value>&page_size=1` once per `result` value (`TestCycleDetail`'s own dashboard mechanism, [ADR-0034](../adr/0034-exec-1-test-execution-recording-dashboard.md)) | Each call's `total` matches that value's seeded count exactly, asserted independently for all four values in the same test — no manual/stale summary, no new aggregate route | P1 | EXEC-1 |
| TC-EXEC-003 | Re-execution preserves history | TestCase already executed once in this cycle | `POST` a second execution for the same `test_cycle_id`+`test_case_id` (different `result`) | `201` with a distinct `id`; both rows independently readable via `GET /test-executions/{id}`; the first row's `result`/`actual_result`/`executed_at` unchanged after the second `POST` — new row inserted, old row untouched | P1 | EXEC-1 |
| TC-EXEC-004 | Status change appends log, doesn't overwrite | Execution exists with result=pass | Correct to fail | New TestLog row appended (`status_change`), TestExecution's prior state not silently overwritten without a trace | P1 | EXEC-2 |
| TC-EXEC-005 | TestLog has no update/delete route | TestLog row exists | Attempt PATCH/DELETE on it | `405 Method Not Allowed` — the `TestLog` path exists (under `GET`/`list`), no mutating method is ever registered for it | P1 | EXEC-2 |
| TC-EXEC-006 | Ordered log timeline | Execution has 3+ log entries | View execution history | Entries shown in chronological order, separate from current-state fields | P2 | EXEC-2 |
| TC-EXEC-007 | Raise defect from failed execution, atomic create+link | Execution with result=fail | `POST /executions/{id}/defects` (external_ref, severity, status) | `201`; immediately-following `GET /defects/{id}` and `GET /test-case-defect-links?test_case_id=` both confirm the new `Defect` (`test_execution_id`/`reported_by_actor_id` correct) and its `TestCaseDefectLink` row exist — not inferred from the `201` alone | P1 | EXEC-3 |
| TC-EXEC-008 | Defect external_ref without live integration | — | Create defect with a plain external_ref string and, separately, a plain URL; also omit it entirely | Both string/URL shapes accepted as-is, no format validation; omitted → stored `null`; no outbound HTTP call made in any case | P2 | EXEC-3 |
| TC-EXEC-009 | TestCase shows all defects, most recent first | TestCase has 3 defects across 2+ different executions, created in a known non-trivial order | `GET /test-cases/{id}/defects` | All 3 defects returned, ordered `created_at` descending, asserted pairwise across the full sequence — not just "all 3 present." A TestCase with zero defects → `200`, empty list, not `404` | P2 | EXEC-3 |
| TC-EXEC-010 | Record-result picker scoped to in-scope TestCases | TestPlan has both a covered TestCase (in an included suite) and an uncovered one (project has ≥1 TestCase outside any included suite) | Open `TestCycleDetail`'s "Record Result" modal's TestCase picker | Only the covered TestCase appears as a selectable option (sourced from `GET /test-plans/{id}/test-cases`); the uncovered one never appears — a UX convenience, the backend's own `422` scope-check (ADR-0033) stays the actual enforcement boundary regardless | P2 | EXEC-1 |
| TC-EXEC-011 | Reject raising a defect against a non-fail execution | Executions with result=pass, blocked, skipped (three independent fixtures) | `POST /executions/{id}/defects` against each | `422 validation_error` each time, never `404`; a follow-up `GET /test-case-defect-links?test_case_id=` confirms no link row was created for any rejected attempt | P1 | EXEC-3 |
| TC-EXEC-012 | `test_manager`/`tester` can raise and view defects without `org_admin` | Actor holds only a `test_manager` (or, separately, only a `tester`) RoleAssignment; RBAC bundle-extension migration ([ADR-0044](../adr/0044-exec-3-raise-defect-from-execution.md)) applied | `test_manager`: `POST /executions/{id}/defects`; both roles: `GET /test-cases/{id}/defects` | All calls succeed (`201`/`200`) — proves the bundle extension (`defect.create` for `test_manager`; `test_case_defect_link.read` for both) actually applied, not just that `org_admin` can reach these routes | P2 | EXEC-3 |

## Governance

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-GOV-001 | Approve a test plan | TestPlan in `draft`, actor has `test_plan.approve` | POST approve | Approval row created; TestPlan → `approved` | P1 | GOV-1 |
| TC-GOV-002 | AIAgent cannot approve | AIAgent actor, any role | POST approve | 403, independent of RoleAssignment | P1 | GOV-1 / RBAC-5 |
| TC-GOV-003 | Approval record survives supersession | TestPlan approved, later superseded by a new version | Query original Approval row | Still present, unchanged, never deleted | P1 | GOV-1 |
| TC-GOV-004 | RiskItem linked to Requirement and/or TestPlan | `risk_item.create` held | POST RiskItem against a Requirement, another against a TestPlan | Each listed on its respective detail view | P2 | GOV-2 |
| TC-GOV-005 | RiskItem structured fields | RiskItem exists | Filter/sort by likelihood/impact | Structured enum values, filterable | P2 | GOV-2 |
| TC-GOV-006 | Attach file to test case | `test_case.update` held | Upload file to TestCase | Attachment row created (url_or_path, mime_type, size_bytes) | P2 | GOV-3 |
| TC-GOV-007 | Attachment size/type limits enforced | Configured max size/allowed types | Upload file over limit; upload disallowed mime type | Both rejected server-side, not just hidden client-side | P2 | GOV-3 |
| TC-GOV-008 | Attachment storage never defaults to third-party SaaS | `ATTACHMENT_STORAGE=local` (default) | Inspect stored file location | Local filesystem/volume, no outbound third-party call | P2 | GOV-3 |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-ADMIN-001 | Classify test case by taxonomy | Seeded TestLevel/TestType/TestDesignTechnique | Edit TestCase, select 1 level, 1 type, 2+ techniques | Selections persist via dropdowns/multi-select, not free text | P2 | ADMIN-1 |
| TC-ADMIN-002 | Design-technique coverage report | Project has TestCases, some with techniques assigned, some without | Open coverage report, filter by suite | Correct percentage, filterable | P3 | ADMIN-1 |
| TC-ADMIN-003 | Generic CRUD list renders from config | Any entity registered in `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` (so `GET /entities/{resource}/schema` serves it), user has `<entity>.read` | Navigate to its admin page | Paginated, filterable list renders with no entity-specific component code | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-004 | Field-type-driven form rendering | The served schema declares string/enum/FK/date fields | Open create/edit form | Each field type renders the matching input (text/select/FK-autocomplete/date picker) | P2 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-005 | Generic CRUD permission parity | User lacks `<entity>.create` | Open that entity's admin page | Create button hidden/disabled; direct API POST still rejected (403) | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-006 | Cross-org 404 across resolver depths | One entity per resolver depth seeded in Org A: `Requirement` (direct), `TestCondition` (one-hop), `RiskItem` (branching), `TestCase` (multi-hop, via TestCondition) | Actor in Org B: GET each entity's item route by Org A's id | All 404 — proves the factory's per-entity `resolve_org_id` chain, not just `Project`/`Release`'s direct-column case, enforces tenant isolation ([ADR-0021](../adr/0021-generic-crud-router-factory.md)) | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-007 | Orphaned TestCase is unreachable, not globally-fallback-readable | TestCase with `test_condition_id IS NULL` and no `TestSuiteTestCase` link | GET/PATCH/DELETE `/test-cases/{id}` as any actor | 404 — proves the factory distinguishes "resolver returned None" (unresolvable, hide it) from "entity has no tenant by design" (global catalog, serve it) | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-008 | Global-catalog routes use has_permission_in_any_org, not the OrgMembership boundary | Actor holds `test_level.create` org-wide in Org A only | POST `/test-levels` (no org context in the request at all) | 201 — succeeds regardless of which org the grant is in; actor holding the permission nowhere → 403, never 404 (no tenant existence to hide) | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-009 | Role org_id-null read/write split | Seeded system-role template (`org_id IS NULL`) | GET `/roles/{id}`, then PATCH and DELETE the same id | GET succeeds (200, via has_permission_in_any_org fallback); PATCH and DELETE both 404 | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-010 | Scope field required on list/create | — | GET `/requirements` with no `?project_id=`; POST `/requirements` with no `project_id` in the body | Both 422 — never an unscoped list of every Requirement, never a create with an unresolvable tenant | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-011 | RESTRICT-blocked delete returns 409 | TestCase has an existing TestStep (RESTRICT FK) | DELETE `/test-cases/{id}` | 409, distinct from 422; deleting an unreferenced TestCase (no steps) → 204/200 | P1 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-012 | RiskItem rejects both-FKs-set | — | POST `/risk-items` with both `requirement_id` and `test_plan_id` set | 422 — rejected at the schema-validation layer even though the DB CHECK alone would allow it | P2 | ADMIN-2 |
| TC-ADMIN-013 | Free-text search opt-in per entity | `Requirement` has `search_fields` configured; `TestLog` does not — and structurally never will (every column is FK/UUID/enum/timestamp, its only payload a JSONB blob), while still having a real generic `list` route for `?q=` to arrive on. ~~`TestSuite` does not~~ — **corrected 2026-09-14 per [ADR-0070](../adr/0070-search-fields-audit-and-numeric-cast.md): `TestSuite` now has `search_fields=("name","purpose")`, so it is no longer a valid negative fixture** | GET `/requirements?project_id=<id>&q=<substring of a seeded description>`; GET `/test-logs?test_execution_id=<id>&q=anything` | Requirement search returns the matching subset; TestLog's `?q=` is silently ignored — **the same result as omitting `?q=` entirely**, asserted against the un-searched response rather than as "a 200" or "a non-empty list" — not a 422 | P2 | ADMIN-2, ADR-0070 (fixture corrected) |
| TC-ADMIN-014 | Field-type-driven input rendering, per type | Entity config with a `string`/`enum`/`fk`/`date`/`boolean` field each | Open the create form | Each field renders its matching input (`FormField`/`select.form-select`/`FkAutocomplete`/date input/`div.form-check.form-switch`) — ~~CoreUI input (`CFormSelect`/`CFormSwitch`)~~, revised 2026-09-08 per [ADR-0042](../adr/0042-adminlte-design-system.md) | P2 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027), ADR-0042 |
| TC-ADMIN-015 | Create button absent (not disabled) without `.create` permission | User lacks `environment.create` | Open the Environment admin list | "New" button is absent from the DOM entirely | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-016 | Read-only entity has no write actions for anyone | Any actor, any permission set | Open the Permission/TestLog admin list | No "New"/Edit/Delete affordance renders regardless of the actor's own grants — config has no `create`/`update`/`delete` at all | P2 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-017 | Stale client-side permission cache never bypasses server enforcement | `usePermissions` cache still shows `risk_item.delete` after the grant was revoked server-side | Click Delete (button still rendered from stale cache), request fires | Server rejects with 403; UI's stale hide/show state never substitutes for the real check (NFR-10/NFR-37) | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-018 | RiskItem scope-selector blocks the list query until resolved | No Requirement/TestPlan selected yet on the RiskItem admin page | Load the page, inspect network calls | Zero list requests fire; selecting a Requirement fires exactly one request with `?requirement_id=<id>` | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-019 | Attachment scope-selector resolves via TestCase | No TestCase selected yet on the Attachment admin page | Select a TestCase | List request fires scoped to that TestCase's resolved chain, matching `resolve_via_test_case` | P2 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-020 | `GET /orgs/{org_id}/permissions/mine` returns org-wide and project-scoped grants distinctly | Actor holds `test_plan.read` org-wide and `risk_item.update` scoped to Project X only | Call the endpoint | Response includes both codes; the org-wide one has `project_id: null`, the project-scoped one has `project_id` set to Project X | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-021 | `permissions/mine` with zero grants is 200-empty, not 403 | Actor is a member of `org_id` with no `RoleAssignment` at all | Call the endpoint | 200, `{"codes": []}` — membership alone is sufficient, no permission code is required to call this route | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-022 | `permissions/mine` 404s for a non-member | Actor has zero `OrgMembership` in `org_id` | Call the endpoint | 404, same NFR-1 boundary as every other org-scoped route | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-023 | TestExecution generic CRUD, one-hop resolver | TestExecution seeded in Org A | Actor in Org B: GET `/test-executions/{id}` | 404 — proves the new `TestCycle`-hop resolver enforces tenant isolation | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-024 | TestLog list/get only, no write route exists | — | Attempt `PATCH`/`DELETE /test-logs/{id}` | No such route is registered (405/404 at the routing layer, not an application-level rejection) — immutability enforced structurally | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-025 | Traceability link tables list/get only, cross-org 404 | `RequirementTestCaseLink` seeded in Org A | Actor in Org B: GET `/requirement-test-case-links/{id}` | 404; separately, `PATCH`/`DELETE` on the same id is not a registered route for any of the 4 link tables | P1 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-026 | Generic admin surface coexists with a bespoke screen | `Organization`/`Project`/`OrgMembership`/`Release` each have both a bespoke screen and a generic admin page | Delete an `OrgMembership` row via the generic admin page (no such action exists on `OrgMembers.tsx`) | Row deleted via the generic surface; the bespoke screen is unaffected/unaware, no duplicate-action confusion in either UI | P3 | ADMIN-2 (ADR-0027) |
| TC-ADMIN-027 | Pydantic-derivable field facts need no per-entity declaration | Synthetic `CrudEntityConfig` over schemas covering `Literal[...]`, `date`/`datetime`, `bool`, `str`, `X \| None`, `uuid.UUID` | Call `derive_entity_schema(config)` | `Literal` -> `type:"enum"` + its `values`; date/datetime -> `"date"`; bool -> `"boolean"`; optional unwrapped to inner type; **`uuid.UUID` -> `"string"`, NOT `"fk"`** (promotion requires an explicit `FieldMeta.ref_entity` — ADR-0055 rejected naming-convention FK inference) | P1 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-028 | Declared `FieldMeta` overrides the four facts Pydantic cannot supply | Config with `field_meta` setting `ref_entity`+`label_field`, `badge_colors`, `label`, `show_in_table=False` on distinct fields | Call `derive_entity_schema(config)` | The `ref_entity` field becomes `type:"fk"` carrying `refEntity`/`labelField`; the hand-picked `label` beats auto-title-casing (`project_id` -> "Project", not "Project id"); `showInTable` is false; badge colours reflect the override | P1 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-029 | Field set is the create/update/summary union, with summary-only fields read-only | Config whose `summary_schema` carries a field absent from both writable schemas; a second config with `create_schema=None` | Call `derive_entity_schema(config)` for each | Union of `create` u `update` u (`summary` minus `id`); summary-only field has `readOnly: true`; `required` true only for a field `create_schema` requires — so a `create_schema=None` entity serves **nothing** required (its generic create was withdrawn; the deleted `.ts` claimed otherwise and was stale) | P1 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-030 | `field_order` reorders without ever filtering | Config with `field_order` naming only a subset of its fields | Call `derive_entity_schema(config)` | Named fields lead, in the given order; **every unnamed field still appears**, appended in derived order. A filtering `field_order` would reintroduce the `Requirement.title` drift this ADR closes | P1 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-031 | Enum badge colours are filtered to declared values, and omitted when none match | Live server; entities with a coloured enum (`test-plans`.status) and an uncoloured one (`entry-exit-criteria`.type) | `GET /entities/test-plans/schema` and `GET /entities/entry-exit-criteria/schema` | The coloured enum carries `badgeColors` containing exactly its own declared values and no others; the uncoloured enum carries **no `badgeColors` key at all**, so `EntityTable`'s plain-grey fallback still applies (UI Design Document §3) | P2 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-032 | Every registered entity serves a complete schema | Live server, authenticated actor | For each key in `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`, `GET /entities/{key}/schema` | Every entity returns `200` with a non-empty `fields[]` and an explicitly-set `label` (never the `_display_name` fallback) — catches a future entity registered without an ADR-0055 port, which no per-entity test would exist to notice | P1 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-033 | `Release` is deliberately absent from the schema registry | Live server, authenticated actor | `GET /entities/releases/schema`; also `GET /entities/not-an-entity/schema` | Both return `404` with the `{code,message,field_errors}` envelope. `Release`'s create/list are 100% bespoke, so it has no `CrudEntityConfig` to derive from and keeps a hand-written frontend config — the single documented exception to "all configs deleted", pinned so a later pass doesn't "fix" it | P2 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-034 | A real admin page renders end-to-end from the fetched schema | Running stack, logged-in admin, a project with a Requirement | Load `/projects/:projectId/admin/requirements` in a real browser | `GET /entities/requirements/schema` is requested; the heading uses the backend-served `label`; table columns, FK label resolution and enum badge classes all come from that response, not a compiled-in object — the only layer where "the served schema drives the render" is a real claim (a mocked hook attests only that the frontend agrees with itself) | P1 | ADMIN-3 (ADR-0055) |
| TC-ADMIN-035 | Sort ascending/descending returns the globally-ordered result set | Live server; 3 `Requirement` rows seeded deliberately out of alphabetical title order | `GET /requirements?project_id=<id>&sort=title`, then `&sort=-title` | Items come back in ascending, then descending, title order — not the insertion order the seed used | P1 | ADMIN-4 (ADR-0056) |
| TC-ADMIN-036 | Sort composes with pagination, applied before the slice | Live server; the same 3-row sorted seed as TC-ADMIN-035 | `GET /requirements?...&sort=title&page=1&page_size=2` | The first 2 rows of the *globally* sorted set, not an independently-sorted first 2 rows of the unsorted set | P2 | ADMIN-4 (ADR-0056) |
| TC-ADMIN-037 | Unknown/unsortable field name 422s, never a raw 500 | Live server, authenticated actor | `GET /requirements?...&sort=not_a_real_field` | `422 validation_error` with a `sort` field error — never an `AttributeError`/500 | P1 | ADMIN-4 (ADR-0056) |
| TC-ADMIN-038 | Schema's `sortable` flag and the route's live acceptance agree | Live server, authenticated actor | `GET /entities/requirements/schema`, read `title`'s `sortable`; then `GET /requirements?...&sort=title` | `sortable` is `true`; the live `?sort=title` call succeeds — both read from the identical `derive_entity_schema` call, so they can never disagree | P2 | ADMIN-4 (ADR-0056) |
| TC-ADMIN-039 | Click-to-sort cycles unsorted→ascending→descending→unsorted; an unsortable column has no control | Mocked `listEntities`, a fixture entity with one sortable and one `sortable: false` field | Click the sortable column's header 3 times in a row (re-querying the header fresh each time); check the unsortable column's header | 3 clicks send `sort=<field>`, then `sort=-<field>`, then omit `sort` entirely; the unsortable column renders as a `columnheader` with no button/testid | P1 | ADMIN-4 (ADR-0056) |
| TC-ADMIN-040 | `TestLevel` seed migration produces exactly the 5 ISTQB CTFL v4.0.1 test levels | Live server, fresh migration applied (no prior manual `TestLevel` rows) | `GET /test-levels` (or a direct `SELECT name FROM test_level`) | Exact-set match against `{Component Testing, Component Integration Testing, System Testing, System Integration Testing, Acceptance Testing}` — no more, no fewer | P2 | ADMIN-5 (ADR-0066) |
| TC-ADMIN-041 | Seed migration is idempotent — re-running it inserts no duplicates | Live server/DB | Run the seed migration's `upgrade()` twice against the same DB | `test_level` row count identical after both runs | P3 | ADMIN-5 (ADR-0066) |
| TC-ADMIN-042 | `downgrade()` removes exactly the 5 seeded rows, leaving an independently-created row untouched | Seed applied; one additional `TestLevel` row created via `POST /test-levels` (not a direct insert) | Run the seed migration's `downgrade()` | The 5 seeded rows are gone; the independently-created 6th row still exists | P3 | ADMIN-5 (ADR-0066) |
| TC-ADMIN-043 | `TestType` catalog ships permanent default rows, idempotent (re-executing the migration's own `upgrade()` body, not just a same-revision CLI no-op), admin-added rows survive | Fresh/cloned DB, `alembic upgrade head` run | `SELECT name FROM test_type`; invoke the migration's `upgrade()` twice via a direct `Operations` context (not a second CLI `upgrade head` call — see TC-ADMIN-041's own sibling note on why that would be vacuous); separately, `create_entity`/`POST /test-types` a 6th custom row, then run `downgrade()` to the migration's own `down_revision` | Exactly the 5 seeded names present after the first apply; the direct-invocation re-run inserts nothing new (still exactly 5); the 6th custom row is untouched by a `downgrade()` — migration only existence-checks/deletes its own 5 literal names | P1 | ADR-0067 |
| TC-ADMIN-044 | Free-text search works on a newly-searchable entity, across a multi-column tuple | Live server; `TestPlan` (5 `search_fields`: `identifier`/`scope`/`approach`/`staffing_and_training`/`schedule`) seeded with 2+ plans in one project — one whose `approach` contains a distinctive term, one matching that term in none of its 5 columns | GET `/test-plans?project_id=<id>&q=<the distinctive term>`; compare against the same call with `?q=` omitted | The matching plan is returned **and the non-matching plan is absent** — a strict subset of the un-searched list. Asserting only that the match is present would pass identically against the pre-ADR-0070 bug, where `?q=` was silently ignored and the *unfiltered* (superset) list came back | P1 | ADR-0070 |
| TC-ADMIN-045 | Numeric `search_fields` column matches via cast-to-text, and filters | Live server; a `TestCase` with `TestStep` rows at sequences 1, 2, 10, 21 (`test_step.search_fields` includes the `Integer` column `sequence`) | GET `/test-steps?test_case_id=<id>&q=2`; then GET the same route with `q=<a digit string matching no seeded sequence, e.g. 77>` | Match half: the step at sequence 2 is returned. Non-match half: **200 with an empty result set** — proving the clause actually filters, and specifically that the route does not raise the `ProgrammingError`/500 a bare `ILIKE` against an `Integer` column produces (Postgres has no `integer ~~* unknown` operator — pre-ADR-0070 both halves 500'd identically, which is why this class had no prior coverage) | P1 | ADR-0070 |
| TC-ADMIN-046 | Numeric search semantics are substring-on-rendered-digits, not exact match | Live server; the same 1/2/10/21 `TestStep` fixture as TC-ADMIN-045 | GET `/test-steps?test_case_id=<id>&q=1` | Steps at sequence **1, 10 and 21 are all returned** (each rendering a `1` in its digits) **and the step at sequence 2 is absent** — both halves required. This is the one case that pins ADR-0070's rejected exact-match-on-numeric alternative: an exact-match implementation would return only sequence 1 and would pass TC-ADMIN-045 unchanged | P2 | ADR-0070 |
| TC-ADMIN-047 | "Columns" button opens the preference modal, listing exactly the entity's table-column fields — no more, no fewer | Generic admin list rendered for an entity whose served schema has at least one `showInTable: false` field alongside its `showInTable !== false` ones | Click the "Columns" button in the table's card header; read the modal's field rows | The modal opens; every `showInTable !== false` field of the served schema appears as a row (asserted by name, each one), and the `showInTable: false` field does **not** appear — both directions, so neither an empty modal nor one listing extra fields can pass | P2 | COLPREF-1 (ADR-0071) |
| TC-ADMIN-048 | Unchecking a field removes exactly that column — header and cells — leaving the others untouched | Modal open on a list rendering ≥3 columns and ≥1 data row | Uncheck exactly one field; close the modal; inspect the rendered table | That field's `<th>` is gone and its `<td>` is gone from every row (`queryBy*` → null, not a count check); every other column's header **and** its cells still render, each asserted by name, so a test cannot pass by removing the wrong column | P1 | COLPREF-1 (ADR-0071) |
| TC-ADMIN-049 | Up/Down reorders a field and the rendered column order follows; first Up and last Down are disabled | Modal open on a list rendering ≥3 columns | Click Down on the first field (or Up on the last); close the modal and read the rendered header sequence; reopen and inspect the first row's Up control and the last row's Down control | The rendered header order matches the new field order as a **sequence**, not merely as a set; the first field's Up button and the last field's Down button are both disabled — both ends asserted, not one as representative | P2 | COLPREF-1 (ADR-0071) |
| TC-ADMIN-050 | Visibility and order both survive a full page reload | An entity list with a stored preference written through the modal (one column hidden **and** two columns reordered, in the same session) | Reload the page fully (a real browser navigation, not a component remount); read the rendered table | The hidden column is still hidden and the reordered columns are still in the chosen order — the same single stored object round-trips both halves, so a write path that persists `hidden` but drops `order` fails here | P1 | COLPREF-1 (ADR-0071) |
| TC-ADMIN-051 | Preferences are keyed per entity — a change on one entity's list leaves another's at its defaults | Two *different* entities whose resource slugs differ but are similar enough to catch a key-prefix bug (e.g. `test_case` and `test_condition`), neither with a stored preference | Hide a column on entity A's list; navigate to entity B's list and read its rendered columns; check the `localStorage` keys written | B renders its served schema's full default column set in default order, unaffected; exactly one key, `testnexa.column-prefs.<A's resource>`, was written — B's own key is absent | P2 | COLPREF-1 (ADR-0071) |
| TC-ADMIN-052 | Locked fields cannot be hidden, and a stored preference claiming otherwise is force-corrected | An entity config with `detailPath` set and a `detailLinkField` (e.g. `Project`/`name`), rendering ≥2 columns | (a) Open the modal, inspect the `detailLinkField` row's checkbox; (b) uncheck fields until exactly one remains visible, inspect that last field's checkbox; (c) separately, seed `localStorage` directly with a preference marking the `detailLinkField` hidden, then load the list | (a) The `detailLinkField` checkbox is disabled **and** checked (visibly present, visibly unchangeable — not absent from the list); (b) the last-remaining-visible field's checkbox is likewise disabled and checked, so the table can never become actions-only; (c) the `detailLinkField` column renders anyway, the stale preference notwithstanding | P1 | COLPREF-1 (ADR-0071) |
| TC-ADMIN-053 | Filter modal: add a condition, remove a condition | Admin list open for an entity whose served schema has 3+ filterable fields; Filter modal open | Assert the initial empty state; click "Add condition" twice; click remove on the first row; attempt an out-of-range remove (index past the end, and negative); keep adding until every filterable field is claimed | Opens with **no** condition rows and an explicit empty state. Each Add appends exactly one row **pre-assigned to the first still-unused filterable field** (first row → first field, second row → second field), never a blank picker. Removing row 0 of two leaves exactly one row **and the survivor is the one that was second** — its own field, not the removed row's (a count-only check would pass the wrong deletion). An out-of-range remove is a no-op, returning the draft unchanged. Once every filterable field is claimed, `addCondition` returns the draft unchanged **and** the Add control renders `disabled` | P2 | ENTITY-FILTER-1 (ADR-0072) |
| TC-ADMIN-054 | Two conditions Apply as one AND-combined map; a field already in use is not offered twice | Filter modal open on an entity with 2+ filterable fields | Add a condition, set its value; add a second, set its value; inspect the **second** row's field-picker options; click Apply. Separately: Apply with a condition whose value was never filled, and with a row carrying a value but no field | Apply emits **one** query-param map (`{title: "widget", status: "draft"}`) — the conjunction the backend `AND`s by construction; **no operator control is rendered anywhere** in the modal (there is no `OR` to express and no operator to choose). The second row's field picker **does not offer** a field the first row already claims, while a row's **own** current field stays selectable (otherwise its `<select>` would have no matching option). A condition with a field but no value, or a value but no field, is **dropped at serialization** rather than sent as an empty-string filter — Apply emits `{}`, not `{status: ""}` | P1 | ENTITY-FILTER-1 (ADR-0072) |
| TC-ADMIN-055 | Cancel and ESC discard the draft; reopening shows the **applied** state, not the abandoned one | Filter modal open with an already-applied filter (`{status: "approved"}`) | Edit the value without applying, click Cancel; repeat, dismissing with ESC; then edit without applying, close the modal, and reopen it; separately click "Clear all", then Apply | Cancel fires `onClose` **and** does not fire `onApply`; ESC does the same (both halves asserted — a discard that also applied looks identical from the close side alone). Reopening **re-seeds from the applied filters**: the abandoned `"draft"` edit is gone and `"approved"` is shown again (asserted as a real close/reopen round trip, not a first-render check, which would pass against a component that never re-seeds). "Clear all" empties the draft to the explicit empty state but takes effect only on Apply, which then commits the empty map `{}` | P2 | ENTITY-FILTER-1 (ADR-0072) |
