| TC-ADMIN-056 | Typed value control per field type; a `text` field is never offered as filterable | Served schema declares `string`/`enum`(with values)/`boolean`/`date` filterable fields **plus** one field of type `text`; also a variant entity whose schema serves no filterable fields at all | Add a condition and read the field picker's full option list; then open with each field type applied and inspect the rendered value control; separately re-seed from a stored filter map naming a `text` field and a field the schema no longer serves; separately render the no-filterable-fields entity | Field picker's options equal **exactly** the schema's filterable fields in config order — asserted as list equality, so a surplus option fails too — and **never** include the `text` field (asserted at both layers: `filterableFields()` excludes it, and it is absent from the rendered `<select>`). Value controls: `enum` → `<select>` with options exactly `["", ...served values]`; `boolean` → `<select>` exactly `["", "true", "false"]` (wire values, not display text); `date` → `<input type="date">`; plain string → `<input type="text">`. A stored filter naming a non-filterable (`text`) or no-longer-served field is **dropped** on re-seed, never rendered as a row pointing at an unofferable field. An entity with no filterable fields renders an explanatory message and **no** Add control | P2 | ENTITY-FILTER-1 (ADR-0072) |
| TC-ADMIN-057 | Derived filtering works on entities/columns that had no `filter_fields`, ANDs rather than ORs, 422s a malformed value, ignores a `text` column, and never crosses the org boundary | Live server, cloned DB (`backend/tests/integration/test_filter1_filter_fields.py`) | Filter an entity that declared **no** `filter_fields` at all, and a column **outside** the old hand-written tuple; pass two filter params at once; `GET` with a malformed value for a UUID column (`test_level_id=not-a-uuid`) and for an enum column (`status=definitely-not-a-status`); `GET` filtering on a `text`-typed column; filter as an actor from a different org; read `GET /entities/{resource}/schema` | Both newly-derived cases narrow correctly — the capability genuinely reaches entities/columns that were unreachable before. Two params **`AND`** (only rows matching both), never `OR`. Each malformed value → **`422`** with `code: "validation_error"`, `message: "Request failed validation."` and a non-empty `field_errors[<column>]` — the same envelope an unsortable `?sort=` already returns, never the `500` an un-coerced value reaching Postgres produces. A `text` column is **absent from `filterFields`**, carries `filterable: false`, and filtering on it is **silently ignored** like an unconfigured `?q=` — explicitly **not** a `422`, since an unrecognised query param has always been ignored on these routes and 422-ing one would break `page`/`page_size`. Filtering never widens the NFR-1 org boundary. `filterFields` equals **exactly** the fields flagged `filterable`, asserted as a derived comparison against the schema's own output, never a hardcoded list | P1 | ENTITY-FILTER-1 (ADR-0072) |
| TC-ADMIN-058 | A real filter narrows a real list in a real browser, and does not survive a reload | Full stack up, logged-in user, a project's `environments` admin list seeded with 3 rows — an entity that declared **no** `filter_fields` before this story (`e2e/tests/filter1-entity-filter.spec.ts`) | Open the admin list and confirm all 3 rows render; click the header "Filter" button; add a condition, point it at `name`, enter the one target row's name; Apply; then reload the list route | **Claim 1** — the unfiltered list shows all 3 seeded rows. **Claim 2** — the Filter button is present at all (pre-ADR-0072 `Environment` declared no `filter_fields`, so it would have been suppressed), and after Apply the target row remains visible **and the other two are gone** (`toHaveCount(0)` — the half that actually proves filtering happened; a no-op filter would still satisfy the "target is visible" assertion alone). Proves the whole pipeline the modal was missing: modal → `EntityListPage` `filters` state → the list query's `queryKey` → `?<field>=<value>` on the wire → fewer rendered rows. The header badge reads **`1`** while the condition is applied. **Claim 3** — a reload clears the filter entirely: all 3 rows are back and the badge is **absent**, the live proof of ADR-0072 §7's deliberate non-persistence | P1 | ENTITY-FILTER-1 (ADR-0072) |
| TC-ADMIN-059 | Row click opens a read-only detail page showing EVERY served field, including ones the list table hides | Seeded `TestPlan` with all 4 long-form fields (`scope`, `approach`, `staffing_and_training`, `schedule`) populated with distinct non-null values; actor holds `test_plan.read` | On `/projects/:projectId/admin/test-plans`, first assert each of those 4 values has count 0 in the list view (the positive control — see Test Design §61); then click the row's body (not an action button) | Navigates to `/projects/:projectId/admin/test-plans/:id`; the detail page renders a label+value pair for all 8 fields the served schema declares — the 4 long-form ones and `created_by_actor_id`, none of which the list table showed — plus the record's own `id`, and exactly 9 value elements in total (8 + id), no more | P1 | ADR-0073 |
| TC-ADMIN-060 | The same detail flow is generic across entity shapes and both route scopes, with no per-entity code path | Seeded `TestPlan` (project-scoped, 8 fields, 5 hidden), `Environment` (project-scoped, 3 fields, 0 hidden) and `TestLevel` (org/global-scoped, 1 field, no project scope) | Repeat the row-click flow on `environments` and `test-levels` — the `test-plans` leg is TC-ADMIN-059's own test in the same spec file, asserting the identical exact-field-count property, so it is not driven a second time here — covering both route shapes (`/projects/:projectId/admin/...` and `/orgs/:orgId/admin/...`); separately render the breadcrumb for an org-scoped and a project-scoped detail route | Each detail page renders exactly that entity's own field count + 1 (9 / 4 / 2 respectively) with that entity's own field names and no other entity's; the org-scoped breadcrumb resolves `Dashboard → {entity label} → Details` and the project-scoped one `Projects → {project name} → {entity label} → Details`, entity segment linked back to its list, `Details` unlinked; `Back` returns to the originating list | P1 | ADR-0073 |
| TC-ADMIN-061 | A row's Edit/Delete controls are not row clicks | Actor holds `<resource>.update` and `.delete` on a seeded `Environment` | Click the row's Delete icon; then Cancel; then click its Edit icon; then click the row body itself | Delete opens the confirm modal with the URL unchanged and no detail page rendered; Edit navigates to `.../:id/edit` (the edit form heading visible) and not to the detail page; a subsequent click on the row body DOES open the detail page — proving the suppression is scoped to the actions cell rather than row clicks being broken for this config; at the unit layer, `onEdit`/`onDelete` fire exactly once with the correct row while `onRowClick` is never called | P1 | ADR-0073 |
| TC-ADMIN-062 | Row navigation is keyboard-reachable, not mouse-only | A generic admin list with a row-click destination wired | Unit: focus a row, fire `keydown` for `Enter`, then `Space`, then an unrelated key (`a`). E2E: in a real browser, `.focus()` the row, assert it is actually focused, press `Enter` | The row carries `tabindex="0"`; `Enter` and `Space` each fire the same navigation a mouse click does (2 calls), and the unrelated key fires nothing (still 2); in the live browser the row genuinely takes focus and `Enter` lands on that record's detail page | P2 | ADR-0073 |
| TC-ADMIN-063 | An entity declaring `detailPath` sends its row click there, not to the generic detail route | `Project` (the one real entity with `EntityConfig.detailPath`, ADR-0060); plus a fixture config declaring one, to prove the branch is on config data rather than the entity name | Open `/orgs/:orgId/projects`, read the name cell's `href`, then click elsewhere on the same row | Both resolve to the identical target `/projects/:id` (the bespoke `ProjectDetail` workspace, heading `Project: {id}`) — one row, one destination; the generic detail page is not rendered. A fixture entity with no `detailPath` navigates to `./:id` instead | P2 | ADR-0073 |
| TC-ADMIN-064 | Detail page state branches: schema loading, unknown entity, failed fetch, Edit gating | Fixture configs, one declaring `update` and one not | Render with the schema in flight; with the schema settled and no config; with `getEntity` rejecting; and with/without `<resource>.update` held, against both fixture entities | In flight → a `role=status` spinner and NOT the "Unknown admin entity" error, with no record fetch attempted (the ADR-0053 regression guard); settled with no config → that error, still no fetch; rejected fetch → an error alert and no field list; Edit is absent both when the schema omits `update` (even with the permission held) and when the permission is missing (even though the schema allows it), while `Back` is always present | P2 | ADR-0073 |
| TC-ADMIN-065 | Detail page opens on an Info tab; an entity with no relationships renders no tab strip at all | Fixture entity with 1 one-to-many + 1 many-to-many relation; a second fixture entity with `relations: []` | Render the detail page for each; read the tab strip and the rendered panel | For the entity with relations: the first tab is "Info", it is the selected one (`aria-selected="true"`), ADR-0073's all-fields list is the rendered panel, and **no relationship list request has fired**; for the entity with none: no tab strip is rendered at all and the field list renders exactly as before | P1 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-066 | One tab per inbound relationship, in served order; a many-to-one fk gets no tab | Fixture entity declaring a one-to-many relation, a many-to-many relation, and its own fk pointing at a *parent* entity | Render the detail page; read the full tab list; then look for a tab corresponding to the parent fk | Tabs are exactly `["Info", <1-n label>, <n-n label>]` in that order; the parent fk **is** rendered as a labelled value on the Info tab, and there is **no** tab for it — many-to-one is excluded | P1 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-067 | A relationship tab lists the related rows scoped to this record, hides the scoping column, and is read-only | Fixture one-to-many relation whose child declares `update` and `delete`; one related row seeded | Open the relationship tab; inspect the recorded list-request arguments, the rendered column headers, and the row actions | The list request targets the related entity's path with `params == {<scopeField>: <parentId>}`; the related row renders; the rendered headers **contain** the child's non-scoping column and **do not contain** the scoping column; no Edit button, no Delete button and no "Actions" header, even though the child's own schema declares both methods | P1 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-068 | Row-click destination differs by relation kind: 1-n opens the listed row, n-n follows `targetField` to the far record | Fixture 1-n relation (row id `s-1`) and n-n link relation (link row id `link-1`, `gizmo_id` `g-9`) | Open each tab in turn and click the row | From the one-to-many tab the URL becomes `/orgs/:orgId/admin/<child>/s-1` — the listed row's own detail page; from the many-to-many tab it becomes `/orgs/:orgId/admin/gizmos/g-9`, the **far** entity's detail page, and specifically **not** `/orgs/:orgId/admin/widget-gizmo-links/link-1` | P1 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-069 | The active tab round-trips through `?tab=`, with an unrecognized value falling back to Info | Fixture entity with relations | (a) Load the page with `?tab=<a real relation>`; (b) load with `?tab=not-a-relation`; (c) load clean, select a relationship tab, then select Info again | (a) that tab is selected, its rows render, and the Info field list is **not** also rendered; (b) Info is selected, its field list renders, and **no** list request fires; (c) selecting the tab puts `?tab=<entity>` in the URL and selecting Info **removes** the param entirely rather than leaving `?tab=info` | P2 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-070 | The derived relationship set is complete: every inbound FK in the registry is either served as a relation or an asserted exclusion | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` | Walk every FK of every registered entity; partition into served relations vs. the declared `EXPECTED_EXCLUSIONS`; check both directions | No FK is unclassified (in neither bucket); no declared exclusion is in fact being served; no declared exclusion names an FK that does not exist; every served relation points at a registered entity and is genuinely listable (the child registers `list` and the relation's `scopeField` is one of that child's own scope candidates) | P1 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-071 | Link tables are classified structurally, not by name, and the three two-FK near-misses are rejected | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` | Compute the structural classifier's set (exactly 2 FK fields, no `create`/`update`) and the set of configs whose model table name ends `_link`; compare. Then check `TestExecution`, `RoleAssignment` and `RiskItem` individually | The two sets are **equal** (a two-way diff, so drift in either direction fails) and equal to the 4 ADR-0005 link tables; each of the three near-misses has exactly 2 FK fields yet is **not** classified as a link entity — rejected for a real reason (a live `update`, a live `update` plus no `list`, a live `create`) rather than for no longer having two FKs | P2 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-072 | A many-to-many tab's label disambiguates from a same-target one-to-many tab | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`; `Requirement`, which reaches `TestCondition` both directly and via a link table | Derive `Requirement`'s relations; collect those whose `targetEntity` is `test-conditions`; then, across every registered entity, collect all relation labels | `Requirement` has exactly 2 relations targeting `test-conditions`, one of each kind, labelled `"Test conditions"` and `"Test conditions (linked)"` — distinct; and **no** registered entity has two relations sharing a label | P2 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-073 | `derive_sortable_fields` and the schema's own `sortable` flags stay one derivation, not two lists | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` | For every registered entity compare `derive_sortable_fields(config)` against the set of `fields[]` entries the derived schema marks `sortable` | The two agree for every entity — the split forced by the ADR-0074 circular-import fix has not become a second hand-kept list | P2 | ADR-0074 |
| TC-ADMIN-074 | Registry completeness is asserted at the **model layer**, so a table with no `CrudEntityConfig` cannot be invisible to both the derivation and its own completeness test | SQLAlchemy `Base.metadata` (every model joins it by declaration) + the real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` | Walk every table holding an FK into a registered entity's table; partition into registered vs. the declared `EXPECTED_UNREGISTERED_CHILDREN`; check both directions and that every exclusion states a substantive reason. Separately assert the literal set of junction-shaped tables (exactly two FK columns, no payload), and that the structural link-table classifier is **not** the `_link` naming convention — the name-based set must be a strict subset, differing by exactly `test_suite_test_case`/`test_plan_test_suite` | No table is unclassified; no declared exclusion is in fact registered; no exclusion names a nonexistent table or one with no such FK; the junction-shape anchor is exactly the 8 known tables; the structural classifier picks up both non-`_link`-named junctions. **Mutation-checked by an automated test, not by hand** (`test_the_partition_actually_fails_when_an_entity_goes_missing`): the partition is re-run against an injected registry with the two junction configs removed and must report exactly those two tables as unclassified, while the real registry reports none — so the two results differ for the right reason rather than both being empty. A completeness assertion never observed to fail is exactly what this story proves you cannot trust | P1 | ADR-0075 |
| TC-ADMIN-075 | The two junction tables registered by ADR-0075 produce real, populated relationship tabs — `TestSuite` had an empty tab strip before | An org_admin, a Project, a TestSuite linked to 3 TestCases, and a TestPlan including that TestSuite | Backend: add the join row through the **real bespoke route** (`POST /test-suites/{id}/test-cases/{case_id}`, `POST /test-plans/{id}/test-suites/{suite_id}`), then read `GET /entities/{parent}/schema`, then fire the exact list request the returned relation describes. UI: open each parent's detail page, click the new tab | `TestSuite`'s relation set is non-empty and carries `Test cases (linked)` (`kind: many-to-many`, `scopeField: test_suite_id`, `targetEntity: test-cases`, `targetField: test_case_id`); `TestPlan` carries `Test suites (linked)` alongside its three pre-existing one-to-many tabs. The relation's own list request returns the join row with the parent id in `scopeField` and the far id in `targetField`. In the browser: `TestSuite`'s tabs are `["Info","Test cases (linked)"]`, the strip is a real `card-header-tabs`, the tab lists the linked TestCases by resolved title, the scoping column is suppressed, and a row click opens the far **TestCase**'s detail page | P1 | ADR-0075 |
| TC-ADMIN-076 | **Amended 2026-09-15 ([ADR-0075](../adr/0075-junction-table-registry-completeness.md) Amendment 1) — the reverse-direction half is superseded; the unscoped half stands.** ~~A junction's scope side is load-bearing: scoping on the parent leaves every other entity's tab set untouched~~, and an unscoped junction list is a `422`, never an unscoped cross-tenant dump | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` + a live server | Against the live route, request `GET /test-suite-test-cases` with **no** scope param, and again with **both** FKs supplied; same for `GET /test-plan-test-suites`. ~~Assert `test-cases`' derived tab set is unchanged by the two new junctions~~ / ~~and again with the *other* FK (`test_case_id`) supplied instead of the scope one~~ — both of those steps are **inverted, not deleted**, and now assert the opposite (see TC-ADMIN-078) | **Still true and still asserted:** a request carrying **neither** arm returns `422 validation_error` naming the entity's own scope field in `field_errors` — keyed on the original arm, so the field name is unchanged even though the message now reads `"exactly one of X or Y must be set"` rather than `"<X> is required."`; a request carrying **both** arms is `422` with `"...not both"` (newly reachable — a single-arm `scope_field` could not express it). **Now false, corrected:** ~~`test-cases`' tabs remain exactly `Attachments`, `Test steps`, `Defects (linked)`~~ — `test-cases` now has **six** tabs, gaining `Requirements (linked)`, `Test conditions (linked)` and `Test suites (linked)`; and ~~supplying the non-scope FK also `422`s (the reverse direction is an enumerated exclusion, not a servable relation)~~ — the reverse direction is a **first-class served relation** on all six junctions and returns a scoped `200`. The two pre-existing tests that asserted those absences (`test_adr74_entity_relations.py::test_registering_the_junctions_did_not_change_test_cases_own_tabs`, and the reverse-direction half of `test_adr75_junction_relations.py::test_a_junction_list_without_its_scope_value_is_a_422_not_an_unscoped_dump`) were **inverted, not deleted**, so the record of the pre-amendment contract survives in both places | P1 | ADR-0075, [ADR-0075 Amendment 1](../adr/0075-junction-table-registry-completeness.md#amendment-1-2026-09-15--every-junction-is-scoped-and-therefore-tabbed-from-both-ends) |
| TC-ADMIN-077 | ADR-0075's two new permission codes are seeded into an already-migrated database and resolve at request time, and the migration's code set cannot drift from the catalog | A live server with migration `7d2c91af4e68` applied; an org_admin | Call `GET /orgs/{org_id}/permissions/mine` and inspect the resolved grants. Separately, load the migration module and diff its `_NEW_PERMISSIONS`/`_NEW_CODES`/`_ROLES_TO_GRANT` against `build_permission_catalog()`/`build_role_bundles()` | `test_suite_test_case.read` and `test_plan_test_suite.read` are both present and org-wide (`project_id: null`). The migration's triples are exactly the catalog rows for the two new resources; every role it backfills is one the static bundles already grant both codes to; `ai_agent_scoped` holds neither (a decision, not an omission); `down_revision` is the prior head. Idempotency is proven by an automated integration test invoking `upgrade()` **twice directly** through a real `alembic.operations.Operations` context bound to the app's async engine, with the precondition (both `Permission` rows already present) asserted first so a `0 == 0` pass is impossible — a second `alembic upgrade head` never re-enters the migration body and would assert nothing (`backend/CLAUDE.md`, TC-ADMIN-041). Mutation-checked: disabling the grant existence-check makes it fail on `uq_role_permission` | P2 | ADR-0075 |
| TC-ADMIN-078 | Every n-n junction lists — and therefore tabs — from **both** of its FK ends, not just its original scope side | An org_admin and a live server; one real row in each of the 6 junctions (`requirement_test_case_link`, `requirement_test_condition_link`, `test_condition_test_case_link`, `test_case_defect_link`, `test_suite_test_case`, `test_plan_test_suite`), each created through **that junction's own real bespoke route**, never a direct ORM insert | For each of the 6 junctions: create the join row via its real bespoke route, read the parent's and the far entity's `GET /entities/{entity}/schema`, then fire the exact list request each returned relation describes — the **original** arm and the **reverse** arm in turn. Separately assert the structural invariant over *every* link entity in `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` (not a six-item literal list): `scope_field` is a 2-tuple naming both FK columns, the pre-amendment arm first, and `resolve_org_id` branches on both arms. Re-run the same inputs through a reconstructed **pre-amendment single-arm resolver** | Both directions return a scoped `200` carrying the created row, for all 6 junctions — `GET ?<original arm>=` is byte-identical to its pre-amendment behaviour, and `GET ?<reverse arm>=` (previously an enumerated exclusion) now serves. The derived relation sets are exactly: `test-cases` → 6 tabs (Attachments, Test steps, Defects (linked), Requirements (linked), Test conditions (linked), Test suites (linked)); `test-suites` → 2 (gains Test plans (linked)); `test-conditions` → 2 (Requirements (linked), Test cases (linked)); `defects` → **1**, from a `"relations": []` empty strip; `requirements` and `test-plans` unchanged. Totals move 22 relations across 9 entities → **30 across 10**, and ADR-0074 §4's exclusions 14 → **8**. `derive_entity_relations` is unchanged — **zero lines** — because `_scope_candidates` already explodes a tuple (`RiskItem`'s long-standing two-parent shape); no new permission code and no migration, permissions being per-resource not per-direction. The mutation check **fails** against the single-arm resolver on the same inputs, proving `branching_resolver` is load-bearing rather than defensive: `_resolve_scope_for_write` passes a `types.SimpleNamespace` carrying only the supplied arm, so a single-arm walk reads `None` and 404s every reverse-direction request | P1 | [ADR-0075 Amendment 1](../adr/0075-junction-table-registry-completeness.md#amendment-1-2026-09-15--every-junction-is-scoped-and-therefore-tabbed-from-both-ends) |
| TC-ADMIN-079 | The newly-served reverse arm enforces the same NFR-1 tenant boundary as the original arm — a new scope arm is a new way into the resolver, therefore a new way to leak | Two orgs, each with its own junction row; an org_admin of org A only; a live server | Against the **reverse** arm specifically: (a) `GET /{junction}?<reverse arm>=<org B's parent id>` as org A's admin; (b) the same with an id that exists in no org at all. Compare both against the identical pair fired at the **original** arm | Both (a) and (b) return **`404`** — indistinguishable from each other, so existence is never confirmable across an org boundary. Never `403` (which would confirm the row exists), and never an empty `200` (which would confirm the route ran and resolved a tenant). Identical outcomes on the original arm, i.e. the widening added no asymmetry. Each arm resolves through the **same** resolver that junction's own bespoke route already uses for that side — composed from the existing `chain_resolver`/`resolve_via_test_case`, never a new walk — so the generic list surface and the bespoke surface cannot drift on what org a row belongs to | P1 | [ADR-0075 Amendment 1](../adr/0075-junction-table-registry-completeness.md#amendment-1-2026-09-15--every-junction-is-scoped-and-therefore-tabbed-from-both-ends) |
| TC-ADMIN-080 | Every link entity declares a `link_create` action and nothing else does — a junction shipping without one renders a tab that lists rows and offers no way to add one, with no error anywhere | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`; `is_link_entity` as the structural oracle (§62's classifier), not a hand-typed list | Partition every registry key: link entities whose `link_create` is `None` (missing), and non-link entities whose `link_create` is set (spurious). Assert the size of the link set independently. Then feed the **same two checker functions** a deliberately broken registry — once with a synthetic link entity carrying no declaration, once with a synthetic config that carries a declaration but registers `create` so it is not a link entity — and assert each reports exactly that injected key while the real registry reports nothing, in the same test | `missing_link_create(ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS)` and `spurious_link_create(ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS)` are both the empty set; the link set is exactly the 6 junctions (`requirement-test-case-links`, `requirement-test-condition-links`, `test-condition-test-case-links`, `test-case-defect-links`, `test-suite-test-cases`, `test-plan-test-suites`) and `len == 6`, so the two set-equalities cannot both pass by the registry having lost every link entity. **Mutation-checked in-suite, not by hand** (`test_the_completeness_checker_actually_sees_a_gap`, `test_the_spurious_checker_actually_sees_a_gap`): each checker returns exactly `{<the injected key>}` against the broken registry and `set()` against the real one — two results that differ for a reason, rather than both being empty. A completeness assertion never observed to fail is the artifact §63 proves you cannot trust | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-081 | Each `link_create` declaration is internally consistent and reaches the wire: placeholders are that entity's own two FK fields, the path carries no `/api` prefix, the permission is a real catalog code, and the key is serialized camel-cased on **every** entity's schema, `null` where absent | The real `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`; `build_permission_catalog()`; a hand-written expected `{entity: permission}` map covering all 6 junctions | For each of the 6: extract `{placeholder}`s from `path_template` and compare to `fk_fields_of(config)`; check the template starts `/` and **not** `/api`; compare `permission` to the expected map; assert that code is in `build_permission_catalog()`. Separately assert that only the 4 resources in `LINK_CREATE_RESOURCES` gate on their own `<resource>.create`, and the other two on a `.update` code that is **not** their own. Then call `derive_entity_schema` for every registered entity and inspect the `linkCreate` key | Placeholders equal the entity's FK field set exactly and number exactly 2 (each id appears once — a placeholder naming anything else can never be filled and would POST a literal `{brace}`). No template carries its own `/api/v1`, which `createLinkRow` prepends (the double-prefix trap `backend/CLAUDE.md` documents). Every declared permission exists in the live catalog — a code no `Permission` row backs makes `usePermissions.has()` false for **everyone including `org_admin`**, a silent total outage. `requirement_test_case_link`/`requirement_test_condition_link`/`test_condition_test_case_link`/`test_case_defect_link` → their own `.create`; `test-suite-test-cases` → `test_suite.update` and `test-plan-test-suites` → `test_plan.update`, i.e. the two REQ-4/PLAN-1 routes keep the gate they shipped with and this ADR re-gates nothing. `derive_entity_schema(config)["linkCreate"] == {"pathTemplate": ..., "permission": ...}` for all 6; the `"linkCreate"` key is **present on every entity's schema**, `None` on each non-link one — never omitted, because `toEntityConfig` distinguishes "no link action" from "an older backend" by the key's value | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-082 | All four new link-create routes write a row that is immediately readable **through the relation's own served list request** — not through a create-response assertion and not through a direct ORM read-back | A live server; an `org_admin`; a Project with a Requirement, a TestCondition owned by a *second* Requirement, a standalone `TestCase` created through the real `POST /test-cases`, and a `Defect` reached the only way one exists (`TestPlan`→`TestCycle`→`TestExecution`) | For each route in turn — `POST /requirements/{id}/test-case-links/{case_id}`, `POST /requirements/{id}/test-condition-links/{cond_id}`, `POST /test-conditions/{id}/test-case-links/{case_id}`, `POST /test-cases/{id}/defect-links/{defect_id}` — POST it, then `GET /entities/{parent}/schema`, find the relation whose `entity` is this link table, and fire `GET /{relation.entity}?{relation.scopeField}={parentId}` built from that served relation rather than from a literal path | Each POST is `201` with a body carrying the parent id under `scopeField` and the far id under `targetField`. The parent's schema advertises a relation for that link entity whose `scopeField` matches. That relation's **own** list request returns `200` containing exactly one row with the far id under `targetField` — so the row the route just wrote is reachable by the byte-identical request `EntityRelationTab.tsx` fires after its action succeeds. A `201` followed by a `404`/empty list is the ADR-0029 resolver-completeness gap a create-only assertion structurally cannot catch. The `Requirement`→`TestCase` case uses a **standalone** `TestCase` (REQ-5's `project_id` shape) deliberately, and the `Defect` case walks the full four-hop project chain rather than a one-hop shortcut | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-083 | A far row in another organization is `404` on every one of the four routes — never `403`, never `422` — and an unreachable **parent** is `404` before the far row is even looked at | Two orgs, each with its own Project/Requirement/TestCondition/TestCase, and (for the fourth route) org B's own `TestPlan`→`TestCycle`→`TestExecution`→`Defect` chain; the caller is a real `org_admin` of org A with **every** permission and no membership in org B | As org A's admin, POST each of the four routes with org A's parent and **org B's** far row. Separately, POST with org B's *parent* id and a random far id. Inspect status and error `code` | All four cross-tenant attempts return `404` with `code: "not_found"` — indistinguishable from a nonexistent row, per NFR-1/ADR-0007. Specifically **not** `403` and **not** the cross-project `422`, either of which would confirm the far row exists. The caller holds every permission, so the far row's tenant is the only thing being tested. The unreachable-parent case is also `404`, decided **before** the far row is fetched. Asserted once per route rather than once overall, because each route walks a different resolver chain to reach its far row | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-084 | Past the tenant boundary the rejections are business-rule ones and are distinguishable: same-org-different-project is `422` with its own message, a repeated pair is `409` | A live server; one `org_admin`; **one** org containing two Projects, a Requirement in project one, and a TestCondition + TestCase in project two; separately, an already-created link row from TC-ADMIN-082's own fixture | (a) POST `/requirements/{req-in-project-one}/test-case-links/{case-in-project-two}` and `/requirements/{req-in-project-one}/test-condition-links/{cond-in-project-two}`; (b) POST the **same** requirement/test-case pair a second time | (a) `422` with `code: "validation_error"` and message exactly `"This test case belongs to a different project."` / `"This test condition belongs to a different project."` — never `404`: both projects are in the org the caller belongs to, so there is no existence left to hide and a `404` would read as "this test case does not exist", indistinguishable from TC-ADMIN-083's cell. (b) `409` with `code: "link_already_exists"`, the pair's own unique constraint surfaced as a conflict rather than a silent second row; asserted on the `code`, not the status alone, since `409` already carries several other meanings in this API | P2 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-085 | A member who can see the tab but lacks the declared link-create code gets `403`, not `404` — and it is the exact code `linkCreate.permission` advertises | A live server; an org with a Project, Requirement, TestCondition and TestCase; a non-`org_admin` member seeded with **both** an active `OrgMembership` and a custom role granting exactly `requirement.read` + `requirement_test_case_link.read` — everything needed to *see* the requirement and its tab, nothing to link it | As that member, POST `/requirements/{id}/test-case-links/{case_id}` | `403` with `code: "permission_denied"`. Not `404`: the caller is a member of the org and can already read the parent, so there is no existence to hide and the only thing missing is the grant. This is also the exact code the UI's own pre-emptive gate reads off `linkCreate.permission`, so a `403` keyed on a *different* code — or a `404` here — would mean the hide-don't-disable gate and the route's real gate disagree, which is the one way this posture can ship a visible button that always fails. The fixture seeds the `OrgMembership` as well as the `RoleAssignment` deliberately: a bare role assignment with no membership `404`s at every gated route's NFR-1 boundary and reads exactly like the route being broken | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-086 | Regression — a **standalone** `TestCase` can be added to a `TestSuite` in its own project, asserted against **REQ-4's own route**, the one that was broken | A live server; an `org_admin`; one Project containing a `TestSuite` and a `TestCase` created through the entity's own real `POST /test-cases` with a `project_id` and no `test_condition_id` | POST `/test-suites/{suite_id}/test-cases/{case_id}` (REQ-4/ADR-0030's route, **not** any of ADR-0076's new ones), then `GET /test-suites/{suite_id}/test-cases` and read the membership list back | `201`, and the subsequent `GET` returns exactly that one case. Before this fix the same call returned `422 "This test case belongs to a different project."` for **every** standalone case, even within one project: `test_suite_membership.py` carried a private three-branch copy of the `TestCase`-project walk written before `TestCase` had a `project_id` column, REQ-5/ADR-0069 added that column and a fourth branch to the *org* resolver, and nothing pointed at the project-side copy — so it resolved `None` every time. Asserted here rather than against a new route because a new route would pass on a codebase where the defect is still live. The case is created through its **real** create route rather than a direct ORM insert, since the defect is precisely about the row shape that route produces; membership is read back through the suite's own route, not inferred from the `201` | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-087 | The four new permission codes and every grant exist against a real **migrated** database, and the live grants cannot drift from the static bundles | A live server with migration `3e6b08c5da71` applied on top of `7d2c91af4e68`; the seeded system roles | Query the `permission` table for the four `<link>.create` codes and inspect each row's `resource`/`action` columns. Then resolve the granted code set for each of `org_admin`, `test_manager`, `tester`, `auditor`, `ai_agent_scoped` by joining `role_permission`→`permission` on the `org_id IS NULL` system roles, and diff each against `build_role_bundles()` restricted to the codes this ADR touches (the four `.create` plus the four link `.read`). Separately, load the migration module and check its `_NEW_PERMISSIONS`/`_NEW_CODES`/`_TEST_MANAGER_BACKFILL_READS`/`_GRANTS` against `build_permission_catalog()`/`build_role_bundles()` and its revision chain | All four rows exist with `action == "create"` and `code == f"{resource}.create"` — the columns are not decoration, `has_permission` and the RBAC-2 admin surface both read them, and a row whose columns disagree with its own code is unreachable through either. `org_admin`'s granted count equals the **total** `permission` row count, so inserting rows without granting them (the specific way this migration could break TC-RBAC-018's pre-existing invariant) fails here. `test_manager` holds all four `.create` **and** all four `.read`. `tester` holds `test_case_defect_link.create`/`.read` and **not** the other three `.create`. `auditor` holds none of the four new codes and still holds every link `.read`. Live grants equal the static bundles for every touched code, so a backfilled database and a freshly-seeded one are indistinguishable. Unit half: `_NEW_PERMISSIONS` is **derived** from `LINK_CREATE_RESOURCES`, never re-typed, and has exactly 4 entries; the three backfilled reads are pre-existing catalog codes ending `.read`, none of them also inserted, and are exactly the link reads minus `test_case_defect_link.read` (ADR-0044); `auditor`/`ai_agent_scoped` are absent from `_GRANTS` *and* from the static bundles' own intersection with the new codes, so adding either later is a deliberate test edit; `tester`'s grant tuple is exactly `("test_case_defect_link.create",)`; `down_revision == "7d2c91af4e68"` | P2 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-088 | Re-running the seed migration's `upgrade()` inserts nothing — proven by invoking the function **twice directly**, never by a second `alembic upgrade head` | A live, already-migrated database; the migration module loaded via `importlib` (a revision hash is not a valid module identifier) | Count `permission` and `role_permission` rows. Open a connection on the app's own async engine, `run_sync` a `MigrationContext.configure(...)` + `Operations.context(...)` block, call the migration's `upgrade()` **twice inside it**, commit. Re-count both tables | Both counts are unchanged. The double invocation is the whole point: `backend/CLAUDE.md` (ADMIN-5/TC-ADMIN-041) documents that a second `alembic upgrade head` against a database already at that revision is a pure no-op at Alembic's **bookkeeping** layer — no `Running upgrade` line, and the migration's `upgrade()` body never re-executes — so a `before == after` assertion around the CLI call passes identically whether the existence-check logic is correct or completely broken, because nothing ran to be idempotent about. A real `Operations` context bypasses the revision table so both calls genuinely execute their SQL, the same technique `alembic/env.py` uses to run migrations against an async driver. **Both** tables are counted, not just `permission`: the migration writes rows to each, and an existence check correct for one and missing for the other is a real, asymmetric bug | P2 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-089 | The URL a relationship tab builds from a served `pathTemplate` is substituted **by field name**, carries no `/api` prefix of its own, and fails loudly rather than POSTing a literal brace | `interpolateLinkPath`/`createLinkRow` (`lib/api/entityCrud.ts`) with a stubbed `fetch`; the real `requirement_test_case_link` template | (a) interpolate with both ids; (b) interpolate again with the SAME two keys in the opposite object order; (c) interpolate with one key missing, and with one key empty-string; (d) interpolate a template with no placeholders; (e) call `createLinkRow` against a stubbed `fetch` and inspect the URL/method/body; (f) call it against a stubbed `409` | (a) and (b) produce the **identical** path with each id in its own segment — a positional implementation passes (a) and silently swaps them in (b); (c) throws an `Error` naming the missing field, rather than sending a path containing `{test_case_id}` and getting an unexplained `404`; (d) returns the template unchanged; (e) the request is `POST /api/v1/requirements/{id}/test-case-links/{case_id}` with **no request body** — both ids travel in the path, and the `/api/v1` prefix is added by `createLinkRow`, never by the template; (f) rejects with an `ApiError` carrying the route's own `message` | P2 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-090 | A one-to-many tab renders a **New** action, gated twice, whose form locks the scoping field to the record being viewed and still submits it | `EntityDetailPage` with synthetic `relations` (a 1-n child registering `create`, an n-n link); `usePermissions` fed a real `{codes:[{code, project_id:null}]}` body; `createEntity` mocked | (a) render the 1-n tab holding the child's `.create` code; (b) render it holding **no** codes; (c) with the code, open the modal, read the scope field's disabled state, fill the remaining required field, submit, and inspect the payload `createEntity` received | (a) `entity-relation-create` renders reading "New", and the link action does **not** — exactly one action per tab; (b) the button is **absent**, not disabled (UI Design Document §5's hide-don't-disable posture; `usePermissions` fails closed while loading, so a disabled-state assertion would pass on a button that later becomes enabled); (c) the scope field renders disabled, and the submitted payload carries `{<scopeField>: <parentId>}` alongside the typed value — two separate claims, because `EntityForm` keeps locked fields out of its Zod schema and merges them in afterwards, so either half can regress invisibly from the other | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-091 | A many-to-many tab renders a **Link existing** action labelled by the FAR entity, gated on the code the backend *declared*, which POSTs both ids keyed by the link row's own FK names | The same synthetic fixture; a link entity whose schema carries a `linkCreate` whose `permission` is deliberately **not** `<resource>.create`-shaped; `createLinkRow` mocked | (a) render the n-n tab holding the declared code and read the button's text; (b) render it holding a plausible-but-different code (the link's own `.read`, the parent's `.update`); (c) with the code, open the modal, type into the picker, choose the far row, submit, and inspect the arguments `createLinkRow` received; (d) open the modal and read the submit button before picking anything | (a) the button reads "Link existing &lt;far entity label&gt;" — `relation.label` minus its " (linked)" suffix, never the junction table's own name — and the create action does not render; (b) the button is **absent**: the code comes from the served `linkCreate.permission`, so a derived-by-convention guess must not reveal it; (c) `createLinkRow` is called once with the served action and `{<scopeField>: <parentId>, <targetField>: <pickedId>}`; (d) the submit is disabled and no request is made until a far row is actually chosen | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-092 | The two gates are independent, a successful link refetches the tab's own list, and the picker's scoping rule resolves all of its cases | The same fixture, plus a second n-n relation whose link entity declares **no** `linkCreate`; `pickerScopeParams` called directly | (a) render the second n-n tab while holding a plausible `.create` code for it; (b) after a successful link, count the tab's own `listEntities` calls before and after; (c) call `pickerScopeParams` for an unscoped far entity, a `project_id`-scoped one with a `:projectId` in the route, a differently-scoped one declaring a `scopeSelector`, a `project_id`-scoped one with **no** `:projectId`, and a differently-scoped one with no selector | (a) no action renders at all — the API-capability half of the gate holds even when the actor is permitted, which is what an older backend or a routeless junction serves; (b) the call count increases, so the new row is actually fetched rather than the table showing its pre-link result set (which reads exactly like the write having failed); (c) `{}`, `{project_id}`, `null` (the caller must render `ScopeSelector` first — the live `TestCondition`/`Defect` cases), `null` again (an org-scoped route has no project in context, so "scopeField is project_id" alone is not sufficient), and `{}` (degrade to an unscoped search rather than a modal with no picker in it) | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-093 | End to end in a real browser: creating a child from a one-to-many tab lands it **under this parent** and appears in that tab's own table | The isolated stack; an `org_admin`; a Project with a REQ-5 **standalone** `TestCase` and **no** `TestStep` — every row asserted must be created by the browser | Log in, open `/projects/{id}/admin/test-cases/{id}?tab=test-steps`, confirm the tab is empty, click **New**, read the locked field's state, fill Sequence + Action, click **Create** | The tab starts on "No records found."; the "Test case" field renders **disabled**; after Create, the new step's action text is visible in the tab's table — which is only true if the locked `test_case_id` reached the real payload **and** the tab's scoped list returns it. A create that succeeded with a null or wrong scope would `201` and simply never appear here | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-094 | End to end: linking an existing row through a membership route that **predates** this ADR, whose declared permission is the parent's `.update` rather than `<resource>.create` | The isolated stack; an `org_admin`; a Project with an empty `TestSuite` and an unlinked standalone `TestCase` | Log in, open `/projects/{id}/admin/test-suites/{id}?tab=test-suite-test-cases`, confirm empty, read the link button's label, click it, type the case's title into the picker, choose it, submit | The button reads "Link existing test cases" and is present at all — which it would not be if `linkCreate.permission` were derived by convention instead of declared, since REQ-4's route gates on `test_suite.update`. After submitting, the far `TestCase`'s own **title** renders in the tab, resolved by the tab's fk-label lookup from the link row — proving the row is readable through the relation's scoped list, not merely written | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-095 | End to end: linking an existing row through one of ADR-0076's four **brand-new** routes, asserted on the wire as well as in the render | The isolated stack with migration `3e6b08c5da71` applied; an `org_admin`; a Project with a `Requirement` and an unlinked standalone `TestCase` | Log in, open `/projects/{id}/admin/requirements/{id}?tab=requirement-test-case-links`, confirm empty, assert the link button is visible, click it, pick the case, and capture the `POST` response while submitting | The button renders — which also proves migration `3e6b08c5da71` ran and granted `requirement_test_case_link.create` to `org_admin` on this database, since the code did not exist before it. The captured response is **`201`** and its URL ends `/test-case-links/{testCaseId}` — the exact path the frontend built itself from the served `pathTemplate`, which is the contract under test. The case's title then renders in the tab. The far row is the **standalone** `TestCase`, so this is also the live proof of `resolve_test_case_project_id`'s REQ-5 branch: before ADR-0076 fixed it, that shape resolved to no project and every cross-project check involving one rejected it | P1 | ADR-0076 |
| TC-ADMIN-096 | ADR-0076 **Amendment 1** — an n-n tab renders **both** write actions, and the two-permission gate resolves all four cells, fail-closed | `EntityDetailPage` with the synthetic `relations` fixture (a 1-n child registering `create`; an n-n link entity declaring `linkCreate`; a second n-n link entity declaring none). The far entity `gizmos` registers `create` and is `project_id`-scoped; a **third** n-n relation whose link entity *does* declare a `linkCreate` but whose far entity registers **no** `create` isolates the API-capability gate. `usePermissions` fed a real `{codes:[...]}` payload per case | Render the n-n tab four times, granting: (a) both the declared `linkCreate.permission` and the far entity's `<resource>.create`; (b) the link code only; (c) the far-`create` code only; (d) neither. Then render two further n-n tabs, each granting both plausible codes: (e) one whose link entity declares a `linkCreate` but whose far entity registers no `create`; (f) one whose link entity declares no `linkCreate` at all | (a) **both** buttons render, side by side in the **one** `entity-relation-actions` strip, the link one labelled `Link existing <far entity>` and the new one `entity-relation-create-link` labelled `Create new <far entity>` — both named by the FAR entity, never the junction — and the 1-n `New` action is absent. (b) only `Link existing`. (c) **neither** — the cell worth asserting outright, because the intuitive expectation is `Create new` alone and a half-implemented gate would render exactly that, producing the `201`-then-`403` orphan NFR-79 exists to foreclose. (d) neither, and the actions strip itself absent rather than rendered empty. (e) `Link existing` **renders** — proving the tab, the schema and both permissions are fine — while `Create new` does **not**, which is what isolates the API-capability half from the permission half; this is the shape 3 of the 12 live link directions really have (`TestCondition`/`Defect` are authored only through bespoke routes while their junctions have perfectly good link routes). (f) **neither** action renders, the other end of the same conjunction: with no declared route there is nothing for the compound action to finish with either — a cell (e) cannot substitute for, since a tab missing both halves at once proves nothing about which gate fired | P1 | ADMIN-8 (ADR-0076 Amendment 1) |
| TC-ADMIN-097 | The compound `Create new` action runs the FAR entity's own create, then links the id it returned, then refetches the tab | The same fixture, both codes granted; `createEntity` stubbed to resolve `{id, name}` | Click `Create new`, assert the far entity's scope field renders disabled, fill the far entity's required field, submit | Four independent claims, each able to regress invisibly to the others: the create call receives the **far** entity's config (not the link entity's, whose two FK columns are the whole row and one of which is the record being viewed); the payload carries the far entity's own scope field locked to the route's project (`EntityForm` keeps locked fields out of its Zod schema and merges them in afterwards, so "rendered disabled" and "reached the payload" are two facts); the link call carries **the id the create returned**, keyed by the link row's own FK names, against the served `pathTemplate`; and the tab's own list refetches. The modal closes and no created-not-linked notice appears | P1 | ADMIN-8 (ADR-0076 Amendment 1) |
| TC-ADMIN-098 | When the link half fails after the create succeeded, the created row is named back and the form closes — and an *ordinary* create failure is handled the opposite way | The same fixture, both codes granted; `createLinkRow` stubbed to reject with a real `ApiError` (`422`, cross-project message) for the first case, `createEntity` stubbed to reject (`409`) for the second | Case 1: open `Create new`, fill, submit, and read the alert left on the tab. Case 2: same, but with the **create** call failing. Case 3: call `createdNotLinkedMessage`/`farRowDisplay` directly | Case 1 — `entity-relation-create-link-error` carries all four things NFR-79 requires and a generic "something went wrong" would not: the created record's **label**, its **id**, the API's **own** reason verbatim, and the plain statement that it is **NOT** linked plus the named recovery (`Link existing <far entity>`). Structurally: the modal is **gone** (a resubmit would otherwise mint a second row for one intent) and `createEntity` ran **exactly once**. Case 2 — nothing was written, so the opposite: the form stays **open** with the input intact, the error renders as the form's own submit-level alert, `createLinkRow` is **never** called, and no created-not-linked notice appears. Case 3 — the message builder is pinned literally, and the label lookup falls back to the id when the create response carries no label field or a blank one, which is why the id is reported *alongside* the label rather than instead of it | P1 | ADMIN-8 (ADR-0076 Amendment 1) |
| TC-ADMIN-099 | End to end: both n-n actions render together, and `Create new` creates the far row through the real route and links it through the declared one | The isolated stack; an `org_admin`; a Project with a `Requirement`, a seeded `TestLevel`/`TestType`, and **no** link rows | Log in, open `/projects/{id}/admin/requirements/{id}?tab=requirement-test-case-links`, confirm empty, assert **both** buttons visible and exactly one actions strip, click `Create new test cases`, assert the Project field is disabled, fill Title/Test level/Test type/Status, and capture **both** `POST` responses while submitting | Both buttons render together — the regression this replaces is "only one rendered". `POST /api/v1/test-cases` returns **`201`** and the stored row's `project_id` is this route's project (asserted on the server's own response, not on the form's disabled input, so the locked value is proven to have reached the payload). `POST /api/v1/requirements/{id}/test-case-links/{newCaseId}` then returns **`201`**, its URL carrying the id the *first* call returned — the composition only a live stack can prove, since the far row has never been read back by anything before the link route walks it. The new case's title then renders in the tab's own list (a `201` followed by "No records found." would be the ADR-0029 resolver gap), and no created-not-linked notice appears. `Status` is filled explicitly to route around a **pre-existing** generic-form defect (`EntityForm` sends `null` for a blank optional field; `TestCase.status` is defaulted-and-non-nullable and `422`s) that is reachable today from `EntityListPage`'s own create modal and is flagged, not fixed, by this amendment | P1 | ADMIN-8 (ADR-0076 Amendment 1) |

Catalog size **110**; `org_admin` holds all four and its bundle equals the catalog; `test_manager` holds all four `.delete` **and** all four `.read`; `tester` holds `test_case_defect_link.delete` **and** the `.create` it undoes, and neither for the other three; `auditor`/`ai_agent_scoped` hold none, while `auditor` still holds every `.read`. The `READ_ONLY_RESOURCES` action-set partition is asserted exactly per resource (`{read,create,delete}` for the four, `{read}` for the other four) plus a standing "no `update` anywhere in the tuple" assertion — the specific claim the two widenings could otherwise have eroded unnoticed, since a link row is immutable (ADR-0005) | P1 | ADMIN-9 (ADR-0077) |
