| TC-ADMIN-122 | `TestCase` → "Defects (linked)": one call links it here — **and** an execution of a different test case links it there, not here | Live server; an `org_admin`, a Project with a `TestPlan` → `TestCycle` and **two** `TestCase`s, each with its own `result=fail` `TestExecution`; no `TestCaseDefectLink` rows | Positive: `POST /executions/{thisCasesFailedExecutionId}/defects` once, then fire this tab's served list request. Negative: `POST /executions/{otherCasesFailedExecutionId}/defects` once, then fire **both** test cases' tab lists | Positive — one `201`, no second call, and the defect is immediately listable on **this** test case's tab, because the route writes `TestCaseDefectLink(test_case_id=execution.test_case_id, …)` and inside the picker's scope that equals this tab's parent exactly. Negative — the same route with an execution belonging to the **other** case links the defect to **that** case's tab and it is **absent** from this one. The negative is written as a first-class case rather than implied: the positive alone proves the happy path and would let a reader conclude the `test_case_id` narrowing is belt-and-braces, when in fact an unnarrowed execution picker would let a user file a defect against a record they are not looking at, invisible in the tab they created it from. That user-visible harm is what turns the widened scope and `parent_filters` from a preference into a requirement | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-123 | The compound path's three refusals, each asserted on its own status and code | Live server; two organizations each with an `org_admin`; in org A, a member holding only enough to *read* the parent tab; a `result=pass` `TestExecution` | (a) org A's admin `POST`s the bespoke create route against org B's parent; (b) the read-only member `POST`s it in their own org; (c) anyone `POST`s `/executions/{passedExecutionId}/defects` | (a) **`404`, never `403`** (NFR-1/ADR-0007) — the caller is a real `org_admin` holding every permission that exists, so nothing but the tenant can explain the refusal, and a `403` would confirm the parent exists across an org boundary. (b) **`403`**, not `404`: the caller is a member who can already read the parent, so there is no existence left to hide — and the refusal is on the code the declaration **advertises**, since a `403` on a *different* code would mean the UI's pre-emptive gate and the route's real gate disagree, the one way hide-don't-disable ships a visible button that always fails. (c) **`422`** — EXEC-3's own precondition, asserted here rather than assumed because it is the entire reason `parent_filters={"result":"fail"}` exists: the picker's filter is a client-side courtesy over a server-side rule, and a test covering only the courtesy would not notice the rule disappearing | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-124 | Compound create, no parent picker, `linksAutomatically: true` — the button renders though the far entity has no generic create | Vitest + RTL; the synthetic `relations` fixture with an n-n link entity declaring a `compoundCreates` entry for this direction (`linksAutomatically: true`, no `parent*` fields) whose far entity registers **no** `create`; `createEntity`/`createCompound`/`createLinkRow` all mocked; both plausible codes granted | Render the tab, read the actions strip, click `Create new`, fill the far entity's own field, submit, and inspect every mock | The `Create new` button **renders** even though the far entity registers no generic `create` — which is the exact fixture shape TC-ADMIN-096's fifth case uses to assert the *opposite* outcome, so this row and that one are the two sides of one gate and neither is meaningful alone. No parent-picker control and no parent hint render. Submitting fires **exactly one** request, to the declared `pathTemplate` with its single placeholder filled from the tab's own scope value, and `createLinkRow` is called **zero** times — asserted as a call count, not as "no error appeared", because an implementation that fired the link call and swallowed the resulting `409` would look identical from outside and is precisely the bug `linksAutomatically` exists to prevent. The far entity's own generic `createEntity` is likewise never called. The modal closes and the tab's list refetches | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-125 | Compound create with a parent picker, `linksAutomatically: false` — the form is gated on the pick, then create-then-link in order | Vitest + RTL; the same fixture with a second n-n relation whose declaration carries `linksAutomatically: false` plus the four `parent*` fields; the compound create mocked to resolve `{id, …}`; `createLinkRow` mocked (rejecting with a real `ApiError` in the last case); both codes granted | Open `Create new`; inspect the body before picking a parent; pick one; inspect it again; fill and submit; inspect both mocks and their order. Then repeat with the link half rejecting | Before a parent is picked, the parent picker (`entity-relation-compound-parent-picker`) and the hint (`entity-relation-compound-parent-hint`) render and the far entity's **own fields do not** — asserted as the fields being absent, not merely as a disabled submit, since a rendered-but-unsubmittable form invites the user to fill in values a later parent choice may re-scope. After the pick, the hint is gone and the form renders. Submitting calls the **compound** route with the picked parent in the path, then `createLinkRow` — in that order, asserted as an order and not just as a pair — carrying **the id the first call returned**, keyed by the link row's own FK names against the served `linkCreate` declaration; a sequence passing ids positionally, or re-reading a stale one, fails only here. Failure case: the created-not-linked path is ADR-0076 Amendment 1's unchanged, so `entity-relation-create-link-error` carries the created row's **label** and **id**, the API's **own** reason verbatim, the plain statement that it is **NOT** linked and the named recovery; structurally the modal is **gone** (a resubmit would mint a second row for one intent) and the create ran **exactly once** | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-126 | The compound permission matrix — including the cell where create-permission alone **renders** the button | Vitest + RTL; three n-n relations on one parent: a compound direction with `linksAutomatically: true`, one with `linksAutomatically: false`, and one with no `compoundCreates` entry for this direction at all. `usePermissions` fed a real `{codes:[…]}` payload per case | Render each of the three tabs four times, granting: both codes / the declared compound `permission` only / the `linkCreate.permission` only / neither | On the `linksAutomatically: **true**` direction the create-only cell **renders** `Create new` — the single most surprising claim in this ADR, and the inverse of the answer ADR-0076 Amendment 1's own matrix gives for a same-looking input. It is correct for a stated reason rather than by accident: no gated second call is ever made, so requiring the link permission would hide a button for a request that is never issued, which is over-gating and not caution. Asserted outright and annotated at the call site, never left to be inferred from a passing happy path. On the `linksAutomatically: **false**` direction the identical cell **hides** it (ADR-0076 Amendment 1's rule, unchanged — a `201` then a `403` would strand a row this tab structurally cannot display). Link-only hides the compound action on both; neither hides everything and the actions strip itself is absent rather than rendered empty; both-codes shows it on both. The third relation hides it in **every** cell — the API-capability half, independent of permissions. The cross is what makes the row meaningful: a matrix tested at one value of `linksAutomatically` would license exactly the wrong generalization in whichever direction it happened to sample | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-127 | The three derivation helpers, pinned directly rather than through rendered modals | Vitest; `scopeArmsOf`, `compoundParentField` and `compoundParentScopeParams` imported and called with literal fixtures | Call each across its own cases: a string / tuple / absent `scopeField`; a template whose placeholder equals the tab's scope field and one whose does not; and a parent entity scoped by the tab's own column, by `project_id`, and by neither | `scopeArmsOf` returns `["project_id"]`, `["test_cycle_id","test_case_id"]` and `[]` respectively — the **tuple** case being the bug it was extracted to fix, a `scopeField === "project_id"` comparison that silently answers *no* for an array and so produces an invisible wrong answer rather than a failure. `compoundParentField` returns the template's single placeholder, and returns it such that the equal-to-scope-field case yields **no** picker and the unequal case yields one — the picker decision is *derived* from this value, which is why a declaration is never allowed to state it separately and contradict the template. `compoundParentScopeParams` resolves its three cases — scope taken from the tab's own scope column, from `project_id`, and `null` where a `ScopeSelector` must be rendered first — mirroring `pickerScopeParams`'s own existing cases (TC-ADMIN-092c), which is the point: a scope field is a query param on `list` and a path/body concern on `create`, so the two must agree rather than grow a second notion of "which project is this" | P2 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-128 | End to end: "Create new" on a `Requirement`'s "Test conditions (linked)" tab creates the condition and it appears immediately as a link row | The isolated stack; an `org_admin`; a Project with a `Requirement` and **no** `RequirementTestConditionLink` rows — every row asserted must be created by the browser | Log in, open `/projects/{id}/admin/requirements/{id}?tab=requirement-test-condition-links`, confirm the tab is empty, assert `Create new test conditions` is visible, click it, confirm **no** parent picker renders, fill the condition's fields, submit, and capture the request | The tab starts on "No records found." and the button is present at all — which it would not be before this story, since `TestCondition` registers no generic `create` and the pre-ADR-0078 gate hid the action for exactly that reason. No parent-picker step appears, because the route's parent **is** the record being viewed. Exactly **one** `POST` fires, to `/api/v1/requirements/{id}/test-conditions` — the URL the frontend built itself from the *served* `pathTemplate`, which is the contract under test — returning **`201`**, and the new condition's description then renders in the tab's own list **with no second request**. That last pair is the only live proof of `links_automatically=True`: it is a fact about another module's transaction body, absent from every interface, so no unit or component test can reach it — and a `201` followed by "No records found." would be the ADR-0029 resolver gap. `priority` is filled explicitly to route around the **pre-existing** blank-optional-enum defect (`EntityForm` sends `null`; `TestCondition.priority` is required by the bespoke request schema but derives as `required: false` from `create_schema=None`), which this story flags rather than fixes for the second time | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0078) |
| TC-ADMIN-129 | The one-to-many gap classification is exhaustive, and the closed set is really closed | Pure-Python: `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` + `derive_entity_relations`, no DB | Compute every one-to-many direction whose child has no generic `create`; compare against the hand-written `CLASSIFICATION` table (closed/open/exception); separately run `one_to_many_directions_without_a_create_path` against the real registry, then against one with a declaration's `far_field` flipped to a different real column, then against one with a declaration removed outright | The real-gap set equals exactly the seven directions `CLASSIFICATION` names, no more and no fewer — a table that named a non-gap or missed a real one fails here, not by inspection. Against the real registry, the checker reports **zero** directions without a create path among the closed ones. The **flipped-`far_field`** mutation is load-bearing: a checker written as "does this child declare anything" would accept it and report full coverage for a tab that offers nothing, so the checker must name exactly the one direction that lost its path; the removed-declaration mutation must do the same. The oracle is `derive_entity_relations`, never a hand-list of one-to-many tabs (ADR-0075's lesson). **Revised same day, pre-merge ([ADR-0079](../adr/0079-child-compound-create-for-one-to-many-tabs.md)'s own `### Amendment 1`, [ADR-0080](../adr/0080-standalone-list-page-compound-create.md)):** `TestExecution` → "Test logs" moved from "exception" to "closed" — three closed became **four**, one exception (`Organization` → "Org memberships") remains | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0079) |
| TC-ADMIN-130 | Each `child_compound_creates` declaration is internally consistent, and none needs a parent picker | Pure-Python: the four declarations, `fk_fields_of`, `app.openapi()`'s path table, `build_permission_catalog()` | For each declaration: check `far_field` against the child's own FK columns, check it equals the template's single placeholder, resolve the template against the live route table, check the permission against the live catalog, and check the four `parent_*` fields are all absent | `far_field` names a column the child genuinely has (`requirement_id`/`test_execution_id` ×2/`test_plan_id`) and equals the template's own single placeholder — the fact that lets `EntityRelationTab`/`EntityListPage` (ADR-0080) reuse an existing modal with zero new JSX, since the tab's/page's own known scope value is exactly what the route needs. The template resolves to a real `POST` in `app.openapi()`'s path table, not a literal, so a renamed route cannot hide behind a valid-looking declaration. `parent_entity`/`parent_label`/`parent_label_field`/`parent_filters` are asserted **absent** on all four. **Revised same day, pre-merge — a fourth declaration (`TestLog` → `POST /executions/{id}/comments`) is deliberately gated on `test_execution.update`, not a `${child}.create` convention**, since no `test_log.create` code exists at all; the "permission matches the child's own create code" claim now holds for **three** of four, with the fourth asserted on its own literal value and cross-checked that `test_log.create` is genuinely absent from the catalog, rather than silently generalizing a rule that stopped being universal | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0079) |
| TC-ADMIN-131 | "New" renders via the declared compound route on a one-to-many tab whose child has no generic `create`, gated on the declared permission — not a conventionally-derived one | Vitest + RTL; a synthetic one-to-many parent/child fixture (`sprockets-no-create`) whose child declares `create` withdrawn and a `childCompoundCreates` entry naming permission `sprocket.author` (deliberately not `sprocket.create`, which does not exist) | Render the tab granted `sprocket.author`; separately render it granted only `sprocket.create` | Granted `sprocket.author`: the "New" button renders with accessible name "New Sprockets", and no "Link existing" sibling ever appears on a one-to-many tab, compound or not. Granted only `sprocket.create` (a plausible-looking but undeclared code): the button is **absent** — proving the gate reads the literal `permission` string the declaration carries, not a `${resource}.create` convention a reader might otherwise assume | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0079) |
| TC-ADMIN-132 | Submitting "New" on a one-to-many compound tab calls the bespoke route, not the generic create, with the parent id filled from the URL | Vitest + RTL; same fixture, `createViaCompoundRoute`/`createEntity` both mocked | Click "New", fill the child's own field, submit, inspect both mocks | `createViaCompoundRoute` is called **exactly once**, with the declared `pathTemplate`, the parent id keyed by the tab's own `scopeField` (taken from the route, never the form), and the submitted field values; `createEntity` (the generic path) is **never** called — the whole point of a one-to-many row's presence in the list *being* the relationship: there is no second call to make, unlike the n-n `linksAutomatically` case this mirrors | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0079) |
| TC-ADMIN-133 | End to end: "New" on a `Requirement`'s direct "Test conditions" tab (not the traceability-link tab) creates the condition with no parent picker | The isolated stack; an `org_admin`; a Project with a `Requirement` and no `TestCondition` rows | Log in, open `/projects/{id}/admin/requirements/{id}?tab=test-conditions`, confirm the tab is empty, assert "New" is visible, click it, confirm no parent picker renders, fill the fields, submit, capture the request | The button is present at all — which it would not be before this story, since `TestCondition` registers no generic `create` and ADR-0078's own mechanism never looked at one-to-many tabs. No parent-picker step appears, because the route's parent **is** the record being viewed (`config === farConfig` on a one-to-many tab). Exactly one `POST` fires to `/api/v1/requirements/{id}/test-conditions`, returning `201`, and the new condition renders in the tab's own list with no second request | P1 | ADMIN-10 (ADR-0079) |
| TC-ADMIN-134 | A live audit of seven flagged standalone list pages sorts into three states, not one binary "has New or not" | Live server; a real project; a minted access token (no credential write) | Attempt a real `POST` against each of `entry-exit-criteria`, `risk-items` (with a valid scope value), and separately inspect `EntityListPage`'s render logic for `test-conditions`/`test-cycles`/`defects`/`test-executions`/`test-logs` | `entry-exit-criteria`/`risk-items` both `201` immediately — already fully working via their own `scope_selector` + generic `create`, zero code change needed, cleaned up after. `test-conditions`/`test-cycles`/`defects` had no "New" purely because `canCreate` never considered `child_compound_creates` (this story's fix). `test-executions` correctly has none (ADR-0079's own open gap, unchanged). A binary "does it have a button" check would have reported the same negative for a genuinely broken entity and an already-correct one that simply requires a scope pick first — this row is what tells them apart. **Forward-pointer, 2026-09-19:** this row's `test-conditions` finding was correct when written — that entity's own standalone list page is retired outright since, [ADR-0093](../adr/0093-retire-test-conditions-standalone-admin-page.md), see TC-SHELL-040/041/TC-ADMIN-144. `test-cycles`/`defects` unaffected **at the time this note was written — `test-cycles` is no longer accurate as of [ADR-0095](../adr/0095-retire-entry-exit-criteria-test-cycles-standalone-admin-pages.md) (2026-09-20), whose own standalone list page (this row's `entry-exit-criteria`/`test-cycles` findings) is retired outright too, see TC-PLAN-018/019. `defects` remains genuinely unaffected** | P2 | ADMIN-11 (ADR-0080) |
| TC-ADMIN-135 | `EntityListPage`'s compound-create gate matches on the resolved scope, not the config's raw `scope_field`, and never fires alongside a working generic create | Vitest + RTL; a synthetic one-to-many child (`sprockets-no-create`) with `create` withdrawn, a `scopeSelector`, and one `childCompoundCreates` entry naming permission `sprocket.author` (deliberately not `sprocket.create`) | Render before the scope is resolved; render after resolving with only a plausible-but-undeclared permission; render after resolving with the declared permission | Before the scope resolves: no "New" button (the `ScopeSelector` card renders instead — structurally impossible for both to render at once). After resolving with `sprocket.create` only: still no "New" — the gate reads the declared permission literally, not a `${resource}.create` convention. After resolving with `sprocket.author`: "New" renders. A fourth, implicit case is covered by construction rather than a dedicated assertion: the fixture's `create` is withdrawn, so the generic and compound paths can never both be true for one config, ruling out the "two request shapes race for one button" failure this row exists to prevent | P1 | ADMIN-11 (ADR-0080) |
| TC-ADMIN-136 | Submitting "New" on a standalone list page calls the bespoke compound route with the resolved scope value, not the generic create | Vitest + RTL; same fixture, `createViaCompoundRoute`/`createEntity` both mocked | Resolve the scope, click "New", fill the child's own field, submit, inspect both mocks | `createViaCompoundRoute` is called **exactly once**, with the declared `pathTemplate` and the parent id keyed by `useEntityScope`'s own resolved field name — not a relation's `scopeField`, since a standalone list page has no relation object at all. `createEntity` (the generic path) is never called — the identical "no second call to fail" reasoning TC-ADMIN-132 already established for the tab context, now proven true for the second host too | P1 | ADMIN-11 (ADR-0080) |
| TC-ADMIN-137 | `test-logs`' `filterFields` grew by two, asserted identically in both places the claim is made | Pure-Python unit (`test_crud_factory.py`) and live integration (`test_filter1_filter_fields.py`) | Fetch `test-logs`' derived schema in both contexts, inspect `filterFields` and each new field's own `filterable`/`type` | Both copies agree: `attachment_url`/`file_name` (plain, bounded strings) are now filterable; `text` (the new required field) is excluded by the same `long_text` rule already governing every other free-text column, not a special case. Written as two corrected assertions rather than one, because the claim already existed in two independent files before this story touched either — the exact "second copy of the same claim" shape `docs/CLAUDE.md` documents, closed in the same pass rather than one now and the other left stale for a later reader to trip over | P2 | ADMIN-11 (ADR-0080) |
| TC-ADMIN-144 | Retiring `test-conditions`' standalone admin surface leaves its backend routes and MCP tools byte-identical — a frontend-only removal proven by re-running pre-existing coverage, not new tests ([ADR-0093](../adr/0093-retire-test-conditions-standalone-admin-page.md)) | Live server; the pre-existing backend integration/MCP test files covering `test_condition`'s generic CRUD routes, `POST /requirements/{id}/test-conditions`, and `tn_test_condition_*`/`tn_requirement_test_condition_link_*` MCP tools | Re-run those files unmodified against the isolated stack after the frontend-only change lands | All pre-existing tests pass unchanged — the claim being proven is "nothing here changed," which a pre-existing green suite already establishes without new assertions; a new test duplicating them would prove nothing additional | P2 | Retire-TestConditions |
| TC-ADMIN-139 | The three real `via` declarations are shaped correctly — checked against the OUTER entity's own scope requirement, not the via entity's | Pure-Python: `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`, no DB | Inspect `test-executions`' "By test cycle"/"By test case" options, `defects`'/`test-logs`' own selectors; for each declared `via`, compare `via.param_name` against the OUTER `ref_entity`'s own `scope_field` | `test-executions`' "By test cycle" declares `via=(test-plan, test_plan_id)`; its "By test case" arm declares none (`TestCase` is `project_id`-scoped directly). `defects`/`test-logs` both declare `via=(test-case, test_case_id)`. Every `via.param_name` matches a real scope field of the OUTER entity it's attached to — asserted this direction deliberately, since checking it against the via entity's own scope field instead is the easy mistake (caught writing this very test) that would silently reproduce the bug this ADR closes, one level removed. Every via TARGET is itself `project_id`-scoped with no `scope_selector` of its own, pinning "no third level" as a checked fact | P1 | ADMIN-12 (ADR-0081) |
| TC-ADMIN-140 | Entities that already work in one hop declare no `via` | Pure-Python: `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`, no DB | Inspect `test-cycles`/`entry-exit-criteria`/`risk-items`'s own scope-selector options | None declare a `via` — a future change spuriously adding one would insert an unnecessary picker step for an entity a user can already search directly, confirmed live before any code changed in this story. **Forward-pointer, 2026-09-20:** `test-cycles`/`entry-exit-criteria`'s own claim here is about their *standalone list's* scope-selector specifically — that surface is retired outright as of [ADR-0095](../adr/0095-retire-entry-exit-criteria-test-cycles-standalone-admin-pages.md), so this row's assertion about them is now historical (the mechanism it describes no longer exists for these two). `risk-items`' half stands unchanged | P2 | ADMIN-12 (ADR-0081) |
| TC-ADMIN-141 | `ScopeSelector`'s cascading render/thread/reset behavior — five claims plus the no-via baseline | Vitest + RTL; a synthetic outer/via pair ("widget"/"widget-owner"), `listEntities` mocked | Render with a declared `via`; pick the via value; inspect the outer picker; search it; click "Change"; render a multi-option selector and switch arms after resolving one | A declared `via` renders as the ONLY picker until resolved — the outer field is absent, not just empty. Once resolved, the outer search's `extraParams` carries BOTH the fixed params (`project_id`) AND the via value, keyed by `via.paramName`. `onResolved` fires only for the outer pick — never for the intermediate via one. "Change `<via>`" returns to the via step and clears the outer value too. Switching arms in a multi-option selector clears a resolved `via` from the arm being left — confirmed by returning to it and finding the via step renders again, not a stale outer picker. An option declaring no `via` at all renders its own picker directly, asserted explicitly rather than inferred from the `via` cases passing | P1 | ADMIN-12 (ADR-0081) |
| TC-ADMIN-142 | End to end: the cascading picker resolves against real data and the exact browser-fired request carries the threaded param | The isolated stack; a real `Requirement`→`TestCondition`→`TestCase`→`TestPlan`/`Release`/`Environment`→`TestCycle`→`TestExecution` chain, seeded and cleaned up after | Open `/admin/defects`, pick a `TestCase` via the picker's own via step, then pick the resulting `TestExecution`; separately, replay the exact request the browser fired directly | The full flow resolves live: via step → outer step → "New" renders, with patient (event-driven) waits rather than fixed timeouts. The replayed request (`GET /test-executions?q=&project_id=&test_case_id=<picked>`) returns the real seeded execution, confirming the `via`-threaded param is exactly what the live route expects. Repeated attempts at all six flagged URLs together under this session's own severe, `docker stats`-confirmed host contention were flaky and reported as inconclusive noise rather than smoothed into a false "all six confirmed live" — this row's own claim rests on the one clean full run plus the direct-request replay, not on the noisy batch attempts | P1 | ADMIN-12 (ADR-0081) |
| TC-ADMIN-143 | A relationship tab's table offers the same sort/Filter/Columns capability a standalone list page has | A `bare`-mode `EntityRelationTab` whose rendered config declares `filterFields` and at least one `sortable` field | Open a relationship tab; assert the Columns button is present; open Filter, add a condition, apply it; click a sortable column header twice | Columns button renders (previously structurally absent from the DOM in `bare` mode, not merely hidden). Applying a filter re-queries with `params` carrying both the new condition and the tab's own scope binding. First header click re-queries with `?sort=<field>`; second click on the same (freshly re-queried) header re-queries with `?sort=-<field>` | P2 | ADMIN-14 (ADR-0092) |

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-TRACE-001 | Full traceability chain view | Requirement has both direct and TestCondition-mediated TestCases, with executions and defects | Open Requirement detail | Both link classes shown, distinguished; latest execution result + linked defects shown per TestCase | P1 | TRACE-1 |

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-MCP-001 | Create/list TestCases via MCP | AIAgent with `test_case.create`/`.read` | Call `create_test_case`, then `list_test_cases` | Same validation/permission path as REST; both tools succeed | P3 | MCP-1 |
| TC-MCP-002 | MCP write attributes AIAgent correctly | AIAgent has `acting_on_behalf_of_user_id` set | Create a TestCase via MCP | `created_by_actor_id` = the AIAgent; accountable human preserved via `acting_on_behalf_of_user_id` | P3 | MCP-1 |
| TC-MCP-003 | MCP/REST schema parity | TestCase created via MCP | Fetch same TestCase via REST | Identical field shape, no divergent contract | P3 | MCP-1 |
| TC-MCP-008 | MCP `create_test_case` permission-denied path | AIAgent with `test_case.read` only (no `test_case.create`) | Call `create_test_case` | `403 permission_denied` body matches API Doc §1 envelope verbatim — proves the MCP tool reuses `has_permission` from `app/core/rbac.py` (not a parallel weaker check); MCP-1 AC1 negative | P2 | MCP-1 |
| TC-MCP-009 | MCP bearer-key rejection (no-enumeration posture) | Any MCP `tools/call` request with no `Authorization` header, or with a non-`tnx_agent_` bearer, or with a `tnx_agent_` bearer for an unknown/revoked key | Each case returns the same `401 invalid_token` body (no distinct code per rejection reason) | Proves ADR-0033's reuse of `_resolve_agent_actor` preserves the no-enumeration posture that ADR-0011 + ADR-0015 already closed on the human side | P2 | MCP-1 |
| TC-MCP-004 | Update TestCase via MCP | AIAgent with `test_case.update` | Call `update_test_case` | Change applied; attributable via audit fields | P3 | MCP-2 |
| TC-MCP-005 | MCP permission enforcement | AIAgent scoped to Project X only | Call `update_test_case` against a Project Y TestCase | 403 | P2 | MCP-2 |
| TC-MCP-006 | Create execution via MCP | AIAgent with `test_execution.create` | Call `create_test_execution` | Row created exactly as EXEC-1, `executed_by_actor_id` = agent | P3 | MCP-3 |
| TC-MCP-007 | Read-only Requirement via MCP | AIAgent with `requirement.read` | Call `read_requirement` | Same data a human sees via REST; no create/update tool exists for Requirement via MCP | P3 | MCP-3 |
| TC-MCP-010 | List agents returns envelope, ordered | Human org member with `ai_agent.create`, 2 agents issued | `GET /orgs/{org_id}/agents` | `{items,total,page,page_size}` envelope; items ordered by `issued_at` ascending; no row includes `api_key` | P2 | MCP-4 |
| TC-MCP-011 | List includes revoked agents | 1 active + 1 revoked agent in the org | `GET /orgs/{org_id}/agents` | Both rows returned; revoked row's `revoked_at` populated, active row's is `null` — not filtered out | P2 | MCP-4 |
| TC-MCP-012 | List agents 404-vs-403 boundary | Caller A has zero `OrgMembership` in `org_id`; caller B has membership but no `ai_agent.create` | `GET /orgs/{org_id}/agents` as each | A → `404 not_found`; B → `403 permission_denied` | P2 | MCP-4 |
| TC-MCP-013 | List agents rejects an AIAgent caller | An `AIAgent` credential, even one mistakenly granted `ai_agent.create` via `RoleAssignment` | `GET /orgs/{org_id}/agents` using the agent's own bearer key | `403 actor_forbidden`, unconditionally — same human-only gate as create/revoke | P2 | MCP-4 |
| TC-MCP-014 | MCP Integration screen: full UI flow | Human org member with `ai_agent.create`/`.update`/`org_membership.read`, logged in | Navigate via sidebar "MCP Integration" link; verify Claude Code/Codex/Cursor connection docs render; issue a key via the form; revoke it | Client-connection headings/snippets visible; raw key shown exactly once (copy-once alert); new row appears Active; after Revoke, row shows Revoked and the Revoke button disappears | P1 | MCP-4 |
| TC-MCP-015 | MCP Integration screen: non-admin acting-on-behalf-of restricted to self | Human org member with `ai_agent.create`/`.update` only — **no** `org_membership.read` (ADR-0063 Amendment) | Load the API-keys panel; issue a key | No member picker rendered (a disabled "You (email)" display instead); the created key's `acting_on_behalf_of_user_id` is the logged-in user's own id, not selectable to anyone else | P2 | MCP-4 |
| TC-MCP-016 | Generic entity tools dispatch correctly across representative entity classes | AIAgent with the relevant permission codes; 3 seeded rows — a tenant-scoped generic entity (`Requirement`), a global-catalog entity (`TestLevel`), a read-only link table (`RequirementTestCaseLink`) | Call `list_entities`/`get_entity` for each of the 3; `create_entity`/`update_entity`/`delete_entity` for `Requirement` only | Each call performs the same validation/permission/tenant-boundary check the matching REST route performs; response shape matches the REST route's own schema verbatim (no divergent MCP-only contract) | P2 | MCP-5 |
| TC-MCP-017 | Method-gating parity — MCP never grants a capability REST doesn't have | `TestCase` (no flat `list` — nested under `Requirement` only), `TestLog` (no `update`/`delete` — immutable), a link table (no `create`/`update`/`delete` — read-only) — **corrected 2026-09-14**: this row was written before implementation; `TestCase`/`TestLog` both gained a real bespoke `create` under ADR-0065 (direct-link/rigor-path create, and the comment-append path, respectively), so "no create" was never an accurate excluded-method claim for either once MCP-5 shipped — the genuinely excluded methods above are the corrected ones **Corrected again 2026-09-13 ([ADR-0068](../adr/0068-mcp-6-per-entity-mcp-tools.md))**: `TestCase` is no longer a valid example at all — it *does* now have a `list` (`tn_test_case_list`), because ADR-0068 folded MCP-1's own nested `list_test_cases` into the tool scheme. The three entities are now `TestLog` (no `update`/`delete`), a link table (no `create`/`update`/`delete`), and `Permission` (no `create`/`update`/`delete` — global catalog, read-only via the factory). | Read `tools/list`; assert no `tn_<entity>_<action>` tool exists for any excluded method on any of the 3 entities, **and** that each entity's *supported* methods do have tools (positive control, so a missing tool proves method-gating rather than the entity failing to register at all). Then call one excluded name anyway. | No tool is advertised for any excluded method — ADR-0068 enforces this by omission rather than the `405`-at-dispatch this row originally specified, which is strictly stronger (a client cannot call what it was never offered). Calling an unadvertised name is rejected by the MCP SDK itself with its own plain-text `Unknown tool: <name>`, **not** this project's API Doc §1 envelope — the request never reaches application code, so §1's parity contract does not apply to it. | P2 | MCP-5, MCP-6 |
| TC-MCP-018 | `_actor_membership_exists` fix — AIAgent reaches the generic factory for the first time, humans unaffected | Two actors in the same org with identical `requirement.read`/`.create` grants: an `AIAgent` and a `User` | Both call `list_entities`/`create_entity` for `Requirement` in the same test session | AIAgent succeeds (pre-fix: unconditional `404` regardless of permission — this is the regression this fix closes); User's own result is byte-identical to its pre-fix behavior (same `200`/`201` it always got) — both assertions in one session, not two, so a fix that only helps one actor type without preserving the other fails immediately | P1 | MCP-5 |
| TC-MCP-019 | Cross-tenant 404 boundary preserved through the generic MCP tools | AIAgent with `requirement.read` in Org A only; a `Requirement` row in Org B | Call `get_entity`/`list_entities` for the Org B row/scope via MCP | `404 not_found` — existence never confirmable across the org boundary (NFR-1), same boundary the REST route enforces, not weakened by the new dispatch path | P1 | MCP-5 |
| TC-MCP-020 | `RiskItem` branching scope enforced identically via `create_entity` | AIAgent with `risk_item.create` | Call `create_entity` for `risk_item` with both `requirement_id` and `test_plan_id` set; then with neither set | Both calls `422 validation_error` — same "exactly one, both/neither is invalid" rule the REST route's 2-tuple `scope_field` already enforces | P2 | MCP-5 |
| TC-MCP-021 | Bespoke-create entity dispatch preserves the underlying route's business-rule rejection | AIAgent with `test_execution.create`; a `TestCase` not included in any suite the target `TestCycle`'s plan includes (PLAN-3 scope check) | Call `create_entity` for `test_execution` against that `TestCase`/`TestCycle` pair | `422 validation_error` — the same PLAN-3 scope-check rejection `POST /test-cycles/{id}/executions` enforces, not silently bypassed by routing through the registry's bespoke dispatch instead of the tool-per-route shape MCP-1..3 used | P2 | MCP-5 |
| TC-MCP-022 | `describe_entity` parity with `GET /entities/{resource}/schema` | AIAgent, any valid bearer key; `requirement` and `release` as targets | Call `tn_requirement_describe`; then read `tools/list` for `release`, and call `GET /entities/releases/schema` | `requirement` returns the identical shape `GET /entities/requirements/schema` returns (fields, methods, scopeField). **Corrected 2026-09-13 ([ADR-0068](../adr/0068-mcp-6-per-entity-mcp-tools.md)), second half only**: `describe` is now generated only for entities that have a `CrudEntityConfig`, so `tn_release_describe` is never registered. The claim's intent — a client cannot obtain a schema for `release` over MCP, exactly as REST cannot — is now asserted as the tool's *absence* from `tools/list` plus REST's own unchanged `404 not_found`, rather than as an MCP error envelope this application never produces for it. | P3 | MCP-5, MCP-6 |
| TC-MCP-023 | Registry completeness — no entity silently missing a tool path | The full `app/mcp/tool_registry.py` registry | Diff the registry's own keys against `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` plus the ~13 declared bespoke mutating routes (API Doc §4) | Every entity/method REST actually serves has a corresponding registry row; nothing in `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` or the declared bespoke-route list is absent from the registry | P2 | MCP-5 |
| TC-MCP-024 | Generated tool surface matches every entity's real REST surface exactly — the diff, not a spot-check | The full generated tool set on the live `FastMCP` instance, plus `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS`, each config's `full_methods` (**not** `methods` — `Project`'s bespoke `get`/`update`/`create` make a `methods` diff false-positive on exactly that entity), and the declared `BESPOKE_EXTRA_ACTIONS` allow-list | For every entity: diff the registered `tn_<entity>_<action>` names against `full_methods` ∪ declared bespoke extras ∪ `describe`-where-a-config-exists, **in both directions**. Also assert: every name decodes back to a real (entity, action) pair (parsed right-to-left, since resource slugs contain underscores); the entity segment is the route module's own literal `resource="..."` string; no name exceeds 64 characters; each action publishes exactly its own arguments; and **no tool anywhere publishes a `resource` argument** | No registry row without a tool and no tool without a row; no action beyond that entity's own REST surface; every declared bespoke extra has an executor behind it and vice versa. Expectations derived from `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` throughout — never a hardcoded list of the 146 names, which would drift with the thing it polices | P1 | MCP-6 |
| TC-MCP-025 | Every pre-ADR-0068 tool name is gone, and every generated tool is usefully described | The live `FastMCP` instance | Assert each of the 8 retired names is **absent** — ADR-0065's `list_entities`/`get_entity`/`create_entity`/`update_entity`/`delete_entity`/`describe_entity` and ADR-0033's `create_test_case`/`list_test_cases`. Separately, assert every registered tool carries a non-trivial description | None of the 8 is registered. Absence is the assertion, not "a replacement exists": asserting only that `tn_test_case_create` exists would pass just as happily with `create_test_case` still registered beside it — the duplicate-surface state ADR-0068 removes, and the one MCP-5 itself left behind for `test_case`. Descriptions are load-bearing at 146 tools (the only signal a client model has for choosing between them), so an empty one fails here rather than degrading silently | P2 | MCP-6 |
| TC-MCP-026 | `AIAgent` org self-discovery — `GET /agents/me/orgs` / `tn_agent_org_list` | One `User` with active/suspended/invited memberships across 3 orgs, an `AIAgent` acting on their behalf | Call as the `AIAgent`'s bearer key -> active-only orgs; call as the human's own access token -> `403`; call unauthenticated -> `401`; call with zero active memberships -> `200` empty; call `tn_agent_org_list` (no arguments) over MCP as the same agent | Agent: `200`, exactly the 1 active-status org, suspended/invited excluded by id. Human: `403 actor_forbidden`, no `orgs` key in the body (no leak). Unauthenticated: `401`. Zero active memberships: `200` `{"orgs": []}`, not an error. MCP call's `structuredContent` is byte-identical to the REST response body | P2 | ADR-0090 |
| TC-MCP-027 | `AIAgent` actor-membership gate fix — 3 previously-`404` bespoke create paths now succeed | An `AIAgent` whose only standing in an org is its `acting_on_behalf_of_user_id`'s active `OrgMembership` + an inherited `org_admin` grant (ADR-0083 bootstrap-fallback), never a `RoleAssignment`/`OrgMembership` of the agent's own `Actor` row | As that agent: `POST /orgs/{org_id}/projects` (`tn_project_create`); `POST /test-suites/{id}/test-cases/{case_id}` (`tn_test_suite_test_case_create`) against a real, `GET`-able suite; `POST /test-plans/{id}/test-suites/{suite_id}` (`tn_test_plan_test_suite_create`) against a real, `GET`-able plan | All three `201`, not `404` — each was `404 not_found` before ADR-0091's fix, for every `AIAgent` caller regardless of real membership | P1 | ADR-0091 |

| TC-SHELL-030 | **Revised 2026-09-09 (SHELL-10, [ADR-0051](../adr/0051-shell-10-project-scope-entity-nav.md)); revised again 2026-09-12 ([ADR-0062](../adr/0062-sidebar-overview-always-renders-at-top.md)):** full project-mode-nav resolution holds on every nested project-scoped route, not just ProjectDetail | Load `TestPlanDetail`, `TestCycleDetail`, and a `/projects/:projectId/admin/:entity` page in turn | Inspect the sidebar on each | All three independently render the full project-mode nav (TC-SHELL-029's assertion) ~~plus "Project Overview" (present on nested routes, unlike the bare project route)~~ — **"Overview" is present on every project-scoped route now, nested or not, not just these three (ADR-0062)** — each its own mount, none inheriting from a sibling | P1 | SHELL-9, SHELL-10, revised ADR-0062 |

Nested patterns: same 2-segment prefix, followed by their existing further segments (Test Plan[, Test Cycle] / entity label[, Edit]) — never the old bare unlinked "Project" label | P1 | SHELL-9 |

No `GET /entities/test-conditions/schema` network call fires for any of the three — asserted explicitly, distinguishing "the frontend never asked" from "the backend refused," since the backend deliberately still serves that schema for the relation tab | P1 | Retire-TestConditions |


## Design System / Shared Components

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-DS-001 | FormField pairs label to input via htmlFor/id | — | Render `<FormField id="email" label="Email" />` | `getByLabelText("Email")` resolves an input with `id="email"` — same accessibility contract as the hand-authored instances | P1 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-002 | FormField defaults to type=text | — | Render `<FormField id="name" label="Name" />` with no `type` prop | Rendered input has `type="text"` | P3 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-003 | FormField surfaces an error via CFormFeedback + invalid | — | Render `<FormField id="email" label="Email" error="Email is required." />` | Input has `invalid`/`is-invalid` styling; `CFormFeedback` renders the exact message text | P1 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-004 | FormField renders no error state when error prop is absent | — | Render `<FormField id="email" label="Email" />` | Input is not marked invalid; no `CFormFeedback`/alert role present | P2 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-005 | FormField forwards ref for RHF register() compatibility | — | Render `<FormField id="email" label="Email" ref={ref} />` | `ref.current` is the underlying `<input>` DOM node | P1 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-006 | FormField spreads RHF register() rest props | — | Render `<FormField id="email" label="Email" name="email" onChange={fn} onBlur={fn} />` | `name`/`onChange`/`onBlur` land on the underlying input unchanged | P1 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-007 | Login.tsx migration is behavior-preserving | `Login.tsx` migrated onto FormField + RHF + Zod | Run `auth-login.spec.ts` unmodified | All existing assertions (redirect on success, `/invalid email or password/i` on failure) pass with zero assertion changes | P1 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-008 | Signup.tsx migration is behavior-preserving | `Signup.tsx` migrated onto FormField + RHF + Zod (5 fields) | Run `Signup.test.tsx`, `Signup.authFlow.test.tsx`, `auth-signup.spec.ts` unmodified | All existing assertions (labels, slug-format rejection alert, exact `signup()` payload, disabled/loading button text, `/login` link) pass with zero assertion changes | P1 | DS-1 |
| TC-DS-009 | Table container renders a caller-supplied header/toolbar slot plus rows | — | Render `<Table>` with a header render-prop and a small fixed row set, page size larger than row count | Header slot content renders above the table; all rows render; ~~no pagination row renders (single page)~~ **Revised [ADR-0061](../adr/0061-table-pagination-always-renders.md):** pagination row always renders now, single page or not — a disabled Previous/Next pair and the page-size selector are present regardless of row count | P2 | DS-2, revised ADR-0061 |
| TC-DS-010 | Page-boundary navigation shows the correct row set on first/middle/last page | Fixture: 25 rows, page size 10 (2.5 pages) | Load page 1; click Next to page 2; click Next to page 3 | Page 1: rows 1-10, "Previous" disabled/absent. Page 2: rows 11-20, both controls enabled. Page 3: rows 21-25 only (not padded to 10), "Next" disabled/absent | P1 | DS-2 |
| TC-DS-011 | Page-size change resets to page 1 (server and client mode) | On page 3 of a page-size-10 view (server mode: `EntityTable`; client mode: `OrgHome` Project table) | Change the page-size selector to 25 | View re-renders on page 1 of the new page size — server mode fires `onPageChange(1)` alongside `onPageSizeChange(25)`; client mode renders the full array's first 25 rows, not page 3 reinterpreted | P1 | DS-2 |
| TC-DS-012 | Backend clamps page_size above 100 to exactly 100; 100 itself is not clamped | Fixture with 150+ rows on any paginated route (generic factory, `org_memberships`, `releases`, `role-assignments`) | `GET ...?page_size=500`; separately, `GET ...?page_size=100` | `page_size=500` request → response `page_size: 100`, exactly 100 items, not 500 and not a `422`. `page_size=100` request → response `page_size: 100`, exactly 100 items (boundary is inclusive, not off-by-one) | P1 | DS-2 |
| TC-DS-013 | role-assignments list route is now paginated with the standard envelope | Fixture: 15 `RoleAssignment` rows in one org | `GET /orgs/{org_id}/role-assignments?page=1&page_size=10`; separately, the same call with no `page`/`page_size` params at all | With params: exactly 10 items, `{total: 15, page: 1, page_size: 10}`. Without params: still the full `{items,total,page,page_size}` envelope (default page_size), never the old bare array shape | P1 | DS-2 |
| TC-DS-014 | Empty list renders the caller's empty state, no pagination row | Fixture: 0 rows | Load the screen | Caller's own empty-state message renders (e.g. "No projects yet."); ~~no pagination row, no page-size selector rendered~~ **Revised [ADR-0061](../adr/0061-table-pagination-always-renders.md):** the pagination row (disabled Previous/Next, page-size selector, a "Showing 0 to 0 of 0 items" summary) still renders alongside the caller's own empty-state message | P2 | DS-2, revised ADR-0061 |
| TC-DS-015 | OrgHome/Dashboard Project table's search and sort survive the container migration unmodified | `OrgHome.tsx` migrated onto Table container, client mode | Re-run TC-PROJ-022 (search) and TC-PROJ-023 (sort) against the migrated component | Both pass with zero assertion changes — only the pagination/page-size chrome changed, not search/sort behavior | P1 | DS-2 |

| TC-DS-017 | Nested table sections remain flat ul/li after outer-table migration (no ARIA regression) | `ProjectDetail.tsx`'s outer tables (Releases/Test Suites/Risk Items) migrated onto the container | Expand a Release row's nested TestCycle audit view (or equivalent nested section) | Nested content is still a flat `<ul>/<li>`, never a `<CTable>`/`role="table"` — `getByRole("row", {name: ...})` strict-mode lookups on outer rows remain unambiguous | P2 | DS-2 |
| TC-DS-018 | Page-size selection does not persist across navigation or reload | Page size changed to 50 on a retrofitted screen | Navigate away and back (or reload) | Screen renders at its own default page size (25 server-mode default, 10 for `OrgHome`'s client mode) — not the previously-selected 50 | P3 | DS-2 |
| TC-DS-019 | Migrated tiles render AdminLTE's own Info Box markup, not either retired shape | `OrgHome`'s 2 count widgets, `TestCycleDetail`'s 4 dashboard tiles, all migrated onto `InfoBox` | Inspect each tile's rendered DOM | `.info-box` root, `.info-box-content` > `.info-box-text`/`.info-box-number`, optional `.info-box-icon` sibling when `icon` is supplied; zero occurrences of `WidgetStatsTile`'s `card.overflow-hidden` or `StatTile`'s `card.h-100.text-center` remain in `frontend/src` | P1 | DS-3 |
| TC-DS-020 | Existing testids and count-sourcing assertions survive the migration unmodified | `widget-project-count`/`widget-active-member-count`/`dashboard-tile-{pass,fail,blocked,skipped}[-count]` | Re-run TC-SHELL-010, TC-SHELL-011, TC-EXEC-002 against the migrated components | All pass with zero assertion changes — same testids resolve to the same logical element, same real-count/zero/error/live-aggregation behavior | P1 | DS-3 |
| TC-DS-021 | Icon-optional variant: icon supplied vs. omitted render distinctly | `ProjectCountWidget`/`ActiveMemberCountWidget` (icon supplied) vs. `TestCycleDetail`'s 4 tiles (icon omitted) | Inspect each tile's rendered DOM | Icon-supplied tiles render a populated `.info-box-icon` block; icon-omitted tiles render no `.info-box-icon` element at all (not an empty one) | P2 | DS-3 |
| TC-DS-022 | Null-vs-loading-vs-error sentinel conventions coexist through the shared `number` prop | `TestCycleDetail` (a cycle with zero executions of one `result`) and `OrgHome` (loading, error, and real-zero-count fixtures) | Load each screen under its own fixture | `TestCycleDetail` renders `"—"` for the zero-execution `result`, never "Loading…"; `OrgHome` renders "Loading…"/"Unable to load"/the real count as appropriate, never a bare `"—"` for a genuine zero | P2 | DS-3 |
| TC-DS-023 | BrandLogo no longer renders the hardcoded "AdminLTE" text (regression fix) | `BrandLogo` migrated onto the mark + wordmark | Render `<BrandLogo href="/dashboard" />` | `queryByText(/adminlte/i)` returns null; the mark (`logo-full.svg`) and "**Test**Nexa" wordmark render instead | P1 | BRAND-1 |
| TC-DS-024 | BrandLogo size="small" renders the mark only, no wordmark | — | Render `<BrandLogo href="/dashboard" size="small" />` | The mark (`logo-mark.svg`) renders; no wordmark text/element present (negative check, not inferred from the mark rendering alone) | P2 | BRAND-1 |
| TC-DS-025 | Logo SVG assets use currentColor exclusively, no hardcoded hex | — | Inspect `logo-mark.svg`/`logo-full.svg` source | Every fill attribute is `currentColor`; no hex/`rgb()`/named-color value anywhere in either file | P1 | BRAND-1 |
| TC-DS-026 | **Revised 2026-09-09 (direct CTO instruction):** AppHeader's `navbar-brand` no longer exists — the header carries no brand mark or link at all, sidebar is the sole brand mount | `AppHeader`'s brand block removed outright (was `BrandLogo size="small"`) | Render `AppHeader` | `.navbar-brand` has 0 count; no `getByRole("link", {name: /TestNexa home/i})` resolves within the header; exactly one such link exists app-wide (the sidebar's) | P2 | BRAND-1 |
| TC-DS-027 | ~~Sidebar renders both mark slots with AdminLTE's own logo-xl/logo-xs classes~~ **Revised 2026-09-10 ([ADR-0054](../adr/0054-tabler-shell-migration-phase-2.md)) — claim collapses to a single mark.** | `AppSidebar`'s brand mount, Tabler markup | Inspect the sidebar's `.navbar-brand`'s rendered DOM | A single `<img data-testid="sidebar-brand-logo">` renders unconditionally, always visible (no collapsed/hidden state to cross-fade into, since `sidebar-mini` — the state this claim originally cross-faded on — is retired); the retired `sidebar-brand-logo-xl`/`-xs` testid pair is asserted absent as an explicit negative. **Historical note, still accurate as of the date written:** the `logo-mark.svg`-not-`logo-full.svg` asset-choice reasoning below (2026-09-09) no longer applies to a Tabler mount (`.navbar-brand` lays the mark and text out in normal inline flow, not AdminLTE's absolute positioning, so the overlap risk it was guarding against doesn't recur structurally) — kept for history, not because the mount still needs the workaround. ~~Both `<img class="brand-image-xl logo-xl">` and `<img class="brand-image-xs logo-xs">` present unconditionally — a DOM-presence/class assertion only; the actual cross-fade on `sidebar-mini` collapse is a live-verification-only claim (see Test Plan's own new risk row). **Asset choice revised in place 2026-09-09 (implementation pass, same branch):** this row (and ADR-0048 §6 / UI Design Document §3.3) originally specified `logo-full.svg` for the `.brand-image-xl` slot. It cannot be used there — AdminLTE positions `.logo-xl`/`.logo-xs` absolutely while `.brand-text` stays in normal flow beside them, so a lockup carrying its own embedded wordmark paints "TestNexa" a second time, overlapping (measured on a live instance). Dropping `.brand-text` to make room is also ruled out: the already-shipped TC-SHELL-005 asserts it visible, so removing it would regress another story's coverage. Both slots therefore use `logo-mark.svg`; `logo-full.svg` is still the login/signup mount's asset (TC-DS-023/030), where it stands alone with no adjacent `.brand-text` to collide with.~~ (superseded mechanism, kept for history) | P1 | BRAND-1 |

## Dashboard & Root Redirect

| ID | Title | Preconditions | Steps | Expected result | Priority | Story |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-DASH-001 | Logged-out visitor at root is redirected to login | No session (no access token, no restorable refresh cookie) | Load `/` | URL becomes `/login`; `Login` renders — no landing content ever paints | P1 | DASH-1 |
| TC-DASH-002 | Logged-out visitor hitting /dashboard directly is redirected to login | No session | Load `/dashboard` directly (not via `/`) | URL becomes `/login` — same `ProtectedRoute` mechanism as every other protected route, no second guard | P1 | DASH-1 |
| TC-DASH-003 | Authenticated visitor at root is redirected to dashboard | Valid session (`accessToken` present), **0 or 2+ active orgs** (an exactly-1-org account bounces onward past `/dashboard` — see TC-DASH-007) | Load `/` | URL becomes `/dashboard`; `Dashboard` renders (org list, or the empty-state CTA) | P1 | DASH-1 |
| TC-DASH-004 | Boot-time initialization race shows a spinner, not a premature redirect | `isInitializing === true` (boot refresh in flight) | Load `/` mid-refresh | Spinner renders; neither `/login` nor `/dashboard` navigation fires until `isInitializing` settles | P2 | DASH-1 |
| ~~TC-DASH-005~~ | ~~Dashboard makes no API call~~ | ~~Valid session~~ | ~~Load `/dashboard`, spy/mock `apiFetch`~~ | ~~Zero calls recorded — page renders as an empty placeholder from static content only~~ | P2 | DASH-1 — **superseded 2026-09-13 by TC-DASH-008 ([ADR-0064](../adr/0064-dash-3-dashboard-org-list-and-chooser.md)/DASH-3):** `Dashboard` now fetches `GET /auth/me/orgs` on every mount, the opposite claim |
| TC-DASH-006 | Reload-while-logged-in still reaches dashboard, not login (regression test for the bug this story fixes) | Valid refresh cookie, fresh page load — `orgContext`/`orgs` unresolved (`null`/`[]`), the known AUTH-2 boot-refresh gap; **0 or 2+ active orgs** (see TC-DASH-011 for the 1-org reload case, which settles on `/orgs/{id}` instead) | Reload `/` | URL settles on `/dashboard` — the guard checks `accessToken` only, so the unresolved `orgContext`/`orgs` gap never blocks this route | P1 | DASH-1 |
| TC-DASH-007 | Single-org user lands on their org directly, no intermediate screen | Exactly 1 active `OrgMembership` | Load `/dashboard` (via `/`, or via post-login redirect) | Navigates straight to `/orgs/{id}` — no card list, no click, no flash of Dashboard content beyond the loading spinner | P1 | DASH-3 |
| TC-DASH-008 | Dashboard fetches the org list fresh on every mount | 2+ active orgs, spy/mock `apiFetch` | Load `/dashboard` | Exactly one `GET /auth/me/orgs` call recorded per mount — not read from `AuthContext.orgs` | P2 | DASH-3 |
| TC-DASH-009 | Multi-org user sees a selectable list | 2+ active orgs | Load `/dashboard` | Heading "Select an organization"; one card per org; clicking a card navigates to `/orgs/{id}` | P1 | DASH-3 |
| TC-DASH-010 | Zero-org user sees an empty state with a create CTA | 0 active orgs (none accepted, or all `invited`/`suspended`) | Load `/dashboard` | Empty-state message (not the literal word "Dashboard" as the heading); "Create organization" CTA present, routes to the existing org-create flow | P2 | DASH-3 |
| TC-DASH-011 | Dashboard's org list survives reload (AUTH-2 staleness regression guard) | 2+ active orgs; page reload, `AuthContext.orgs` unresolved (`null`/`[]`) after the boot-time silent refresh | Reload `/dashboard` | Org list still renders correctly — fetched fresh via `GET /auth/me/orgs`, not read from the stale/empty `AuthContext.orgs` | P1 | DASH-3 |
| TC-DASH-012 | `/orgs/pick` no longer resolves to `OrgPicker` | Any authenticated user | Navigate to `/orgs/pick` directly | `OrgPicker`'s own distinguishing content is absent — the route is retired, not merely re-labeled | P2 | DASH-3 |
| TC-DASH-013 | Post-login/signup/accept-invite redirect always targets `/dashboard` first | Fresh login, tested at 1 and 2+ active orgs (**0 is unreachable here — corrected 2026-09-14**: `POST /auth/login` itself returns `403 no_active_organization` for zero active memberships, per AUTH-1, before any redirect logic ever runs; signup/accept-invite are structurally always exactly 1 org — a signup mints its own new org, an accepted invite joins exactly the one org it was issued for — so 0/2+ don't apply to either method at all, only to login) | Complete auth | Lands on `/dashboard` (then DASH-3's own count-branching takes over — e.g. a 1-org account bounces onward immediately); `Login.tsx`/`Signup.tsx`/`AcceptInvite.tsx` no longer branch to `/orgs/pick` or a direct org URL themselves | P1 | DASH-3 |

**ADMIN-3 (2026-09-09, [ADR-0055](../adr/0055-admin-3-backend-driven-entity-schema.md) — the backend becomes the single source of truth for admin-CRUD field shape):** written against a **248/143** base that predates Tabler-1/Tabler-2 (this branch and the Tabler stories diverged from the same pre-merge point and neither could see the other's in-flight totals); reconciled at merge time (2026-09-11) against the actual current base above. Taxonomy & Generic Admin CRUD extends from 26/16 to **34/22** with eight new rows, TC-ADMIN-027..034 — that row itself is untouched by either Tabler pass, so the same `+8/+6` delta applies unchanged, just against the Tabler-2 total instead of the stale pre-merge one. Grand total **248/143 → 256/149** by direct column summation (34+39+25+9+16+12+8+34+5+7+29+32+6 = 256; P1 22+23+12+7+11+7+3+22+4+0+16+19+3 = 149), the struck-through Landing Page row still excluded.

The eight split across all three test layers rather than clustering in one, because the classes genuinely live at different levels (Test Design §46, renumbered from §44 for the same merge-collision reason): TC-ADMIN-027..030 are **backend unit** (derivation logic against synthetic schemas — deliberately not the 27 real entities, so each row pins the *rule* rather than one entity's incidental field list); TC-ADMIN-031..033 are **backend integration** against the live route; TC-ADMIN-034 is **e2e**, and can only be e2e — a mocked `useEntitySchema` attests that the frontend agrees with *itself*, which is precisely the thing this ADR does not need proving (see `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s hand-typed-mock note).

Three of the eight are negative/absence assertions, which is where this feature's real risk sits: TC-ADMIN-027's `uuid.UUID` → `"string"` (not `"fk"`), TC-ADMIN-030's "an unnamed field still appears" (a filtering `field_order` would reintroduce the very `Requirement.title` drift this ADR closes), and TC-ADMIN-033's deliberate `404` for `Release`. None of these would fail loudly if regressed — they'd surface as a raw UUID in a column, a silently-missing form field, or an entity quietly registered behind a factory that can't serve it — so each is pinned explicitly rather than inferred from its positive sibling.

**No existing TC-ADMIN row is struck through or superseded by this pass.** TC-ADMIN-003/004 (and the rest of the ADMIN-2 set) assert *behaviour* of the generic admin surface — field types render correctly, permissions hide/disable actions, scope selectors resolve — and every one of those claims is still exactly true; only *where the field list comes from* changed. Two of them named the old mechanism in their **Preconditions** cell specifically (TC-ADMIN-003's "a registered `entityConfigs` entry", TC-ADMIN-004's "Entity config declares…"), so those two cells are **corrected in place** — to `ALL_ENTITY_CONFIGS` / "the served schema" respectively — rather than struck: the row's own assertion (its Steps and Expected-result cells) is unchanged and still passes verbatim, and a strikethrough would wrongly imply the behaviour it pins had stopped being required. This is the "correct in place when the claim is still true, strike only when it has become false" distinction `docs/CLAUDE.md` draws.

**Numbering note:** this story originally claimed `ADR-0053`/`11.36`/`§44`/`NFR-56`+`NFR-57` — the same numbers Tabler-1/Tabler-2 each independently claimed on `origin/main` while this branch was in flight, found at merge time (2026-09-11); renumbered to `ADR-0055`/`11.38`/`§46`/`NFR-58`+`NFR-59` (`TC-ADMIN-027..034` unaffected, no collision in that series).

**A fifth independent instance of this file's stale-count class, found by this pass and fixed here — this time in a per-area P1 *cell*, not the printed Total row.** The four already recorded above all had the same shape (a row updated, the Total never bumped); this one is the inverse, and the existing `docs/CLAUDE.md` rule ("sum every row's literal value") would never have caught it, because the literal values summed correctly to the printed total both before and after. It surfaced only from a second, independent check this pass ran on its own row: counting the actual `| TC-ADMIN-… |` lines in the section above. That count is **49 rows, of which 30 carry `P1`** — the 49 matches this row's test-case count exactly (43 before + 6 new), confirming this area's count column is a straight row count and that the same should hold for its P1 column; but 30 − 3 new P1s = **27** pre-existing P1 rows, against the **26** the table had printed since ADMIN-5's pass. Corrected to 30 here, with the grand total following to 168. **Which earlier pass dropped the one is not determinable from the notes above and was not chased** — every pass from ADR-0027's onward states an internally-consistent delta, so the error is in a starting point, exactly as the REQ-5/MCP-6 reconciliation above found for `TC-MCP-`.

**Out-of-scope drift flagged, deliberately not fixed here:** the same actual-row-count check run across *every* feature area (not just this pass's own) shows several other areas whose printed values disagree with a naive count of their live rows — Auth (35 rows vs. 34 printed), Requirement & Test Case Authoring (19 vs. 14), Layout & Navigation (39 rows, 11 carrying a strikethrough somewhere, vs. 28 printed), Project & Release (27 rows, 5 struck, vs. 21 printed). These are **not** all errors: at least some are the intended effect of this file's own supersession convention (`docs/CLAUDE.md` — struck rows stay in the table and are excluded from counts), and distinguishing "correctly excluded struck row" from "genuinely dropped count" needs a row-by-row read of each area's own history, which is a dedicated audit rather than a clause in a feature pass. Recorded here so the next pass starts from a known-open question instead of rediscovering it; this pass corrected only the area it actually touched and could verify end-to-end.

The three P1s are chosen by failure *consequence*, not by feature centrality: **TC-ADMIN-048** (a hide that removes the wrong column, or leaves cells misaligned with headers, silently misattributes data — a wrong value under a right header is worse than a missing column), **TC-ADMIN-050** (persistence is the whole point of the feature; a preference that doesn't survive a reload means every viewer re-hides the same column forever, and the failure is invisible in any mocked test — see Test Design §59), and **TC-ADMIN-052** (a viewer who hides `detailLinkField` loses the only navigation into the entity's detail workspace, [ADR-0060](../adr/0060-projects-page-retired-generic-surface.md), with no error and no obvious recovery — this is the one row that guards against a genuinely stranding state). The three P2s (TC-ADMIN-047 modal population, TC-ADMIN-049 reorder + end-boundaries, TC-ADMIN-051 per-entity key isolation) each pin a real claim whose failure is annoying and visible rather than silently destructive.

**All six are frontend rows and that is deliberate, not a coverage gap** — ADR-0071 has no backend surface at all (no route, no schema field, no migration, no permission code), so a backend-unit or backend-integration row would have nothing to assert. Within the frontend, TC-ADMIN-050's own claim ("survives a full page reload") can only be honoured by a real browser: a Vitest assertion that `saveColumnPreferences` wrote and `loadColumnPreferences` read proves the two halves agree with each other, which is precisely the thing needing no proof (`frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s hand-typed-mock note, the same reasoning TC-ADMIN-034 applied one story earlier). **No existing TC-ADMIN row is struck through or superseded by this pass** — every prior claim about the generic admin surface's rendering, permissions, scope selection and sort behaviour is still exactly true; this pass adds a control alongside them and changes none of their assertions.

**Numbering note:** `TC-ADMIN-044..046` (along with `ADR-0070`/WBS `11.50`/`§58`/`NFR-70`) are claimed by the sibling `search-fields-audit` branch, unmerged and in flight when this pass was written — so this story's rows start at **047**, chosen next-free *above* that branch's claims rather than next-free against `origin/main` alone. Root `CLAUDE.md`'s 7th recorded numbering-collision instance was caused by exactly the opposite reading (treating an in-flight sibling's claim as invisible); re-check both `origin/main` and that branch immediately before merging.

**Docs written concurrently with the implementation on this same branch** (`frontend/src/lib/columnPreferences.ts`, the `ColumnPreferencesModal` organism, `EntityTable`'s "Columns" header button) — the note originally written here said the automated tests were a follow-on step, which was true at the moment it was written and is **no longer true**; corrected in place the same pass rather than left to go stale (`docs/CLAUDE.md`'s own "a docs-only pass's status phrases go stale the moment a later same-branch commit lands the code" note).

**Coverage as landed: all six rows have passing automated tests.** Three files, chosen so each TC's claim is asserted at the layer that can actually falsify it:

- `frontend/src/lib/columnPreferences.test.ts` — the merge/ordering/locking/degradation rules as pure data (TC-ADMIN-050's storage round-trip, TC-ADMIN-051's per-entity keying **including prefix-similar slugs** per that row's own literal precondition, TC-ADMIN-052's force-restore of a locked field, NFR-71's corrupt/unavailable-storage fallbacks).
- `frontend/src/components/organisms/column-preferences-modal/column-preferences-modal.test.tsx` — modal population, toggle, reorder, both end-of-list disabled boundaries, both lock reasons, draft discard-on-cancel (TC-ADMIN-047/048/049/052).
- `frontend/src/components/organisms/entity-table.columnPreferences.test.tsx` — the integration seam: a preference chosen in the modal actually changes the painted columns *and their cells*, reaches `localStorage`, and is still in force after a full unmount/remount.
- `e2e/tests/colpref1-column-preferences.spec.ts` — TC-ADMIN-050's own "full page reload" claim, which no Vitest assertion can honour (jsdom's `localStorage` is a per-test in-memory shim and nothing there re-executes the app bundle). Includes a dedicated case proving a hidden column **and** a reorder made in one session both survive one reload from a single stored object, matching that row's literal precondition rather than the two easier one-half-each cases beside it.
